## T2. Quantum sensing and learning of classical signals and Hamiltonians

### I-20261012-02: Single-qubit sensing of weak classical signals below the classical noise floor

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch A |
| Hypothesis | A single controllable qubit coupled to a sensor learns Fourier amplitudes and temporal correlations of a weak classical signal with exponentially fewer measurements than any classical sensing protocol. |
| Mechanism | Kannan et al. prove quantum phase-space inference with a single squeezed mode or qubit gives exponential reductions in measurement count, without entanglement; the batch claims the paper demonstrates a 10^7-fold reduction on a superconducting cavity-qubit device. |
| Data | Classical signal coupled to a qubit sensor; learner sees qubit measurement outcomes. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | Simulate 1-2 sensor qubits plus small ancilla register (<=5 qubits) coupled to a weak classical oscillator; baselines: matched filter, maximum likelihood from homodyne data, small NN; metric: measurements to estimate \|alpha\| within 10%; success: quantum <=100 vs classical >=1e4. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Asymptotic advantage, constants may dominate; preprint. |
| References cited | 2608.13521, 2112.00778 |
| Reference checks | 2608.13521: **OK** (Exists (Kannan, Prabhu, Khan, Sohoni, ..., McMahon, Cotler). The exponential reduction and the 10^7 figure are in the paper. Distortions in the card: (i) 'single squeezed mode or qubit' merges Thm 1 (bosonic mode + one ancilla qubit vs Gaussian protocols at equal energy) and Thm 2 (squeezed Gaussian vs coherent-state probes); (ii) 'without entanglement' means no entangled multimode probe, while the ancilla qubit IS entangled with the sensor mode through echoed conditional displacements; (iii) 'than any classical sensing protocol' is wrong for Thm 1, whose comparison class is Gaussian quantum sensors; (iv) the ~100 shots at k=20 are measured, but the Gaussian side's requirement comes from simulation or the lower bound, not from a measurement.); 2112.00778: **OK** (Exists (Huang, Broughton, Cotler, Chen, Li, Mohseni, ...). Abstract read: exponential advantage in learning from experiments with quantum memory. Context only: it does not cover classical signals or sensors.) |
| Technical problems | Wrong task in the minimal test. 'Estimate \|alpha\| within 10%' is a first-moment displacement-sensing task, where quantum resources give at most constant or quadratic gains. The exponential separation concerns the k-th phase-space Fourier coefficient E[e^{ikx}] of the displacement DISTRIBUTION, at large k and fixed probe energy.; The size of the gap depends on energy. In the paper's Lemma 6 protocol (weight p=4E/k^2, branch displaced by beta=-ik/2), one-qubit control needs about 1/(4p(1-p)) ~ k^2/(16E) queries per unit precision. Squeezed homodyne at the same mean photon number E (x-noise variance v=e^{-2r}/4, sinh^2 r=E) needs about e^{k^2 v} ~ e^{k^2/(16E)}. These scalings are our derivation in the paper's conventions. At E=k=20 the ratio is only about 2; the 10^7 gap needs E of about 1-2 photons at k=20. The paper says a Gaussian protocol matches with polynomially more energy.; The success threshold 'quantum <=100 vs classical >=1e4' is arbitrary and ignores both k and E.; All proposed baselines (matched filter, ML from homodyne data, small NN) post-process Gaussian measurements, so the AFI lower bound caps them on the worst-case family. Their failure there is fixed by theory; the test only becomes informative if the signal family is varied.; Fairness subtlety: the comparison is at equal MEAN photon number. The qubit protocol prepares an unbalanced cat whose rare branch holds ~k^2/4 photons. The restriction is Gaussian vs non-Gaussian technology, not measure-then-process vs coherent processing (that version is Thm 3, the memory qubit).; The ancilla register of up to 5 qubits is unnecessary: Thm 1 uses one qubit and one control operation. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 partially tripped: Kannan et al. already prove and demonstrate the worst-case separation; the idea survives only as a replication of a known separation plus a new structured-signal test, K4 tripped by the original minimal test (success threshold not tied to k or to the probe energy; baselines all bounded by the theorem); repaired in the decisive test, K5: qubit readout error, ancilla dephasing and cavity loss must be modelled; the gap is exp(~k^2/16E), so realistic squeezing caps decide the regime, K1, K2, K3, K6 not tripped: quantum data from a sensor, no linear algebra, fixed non-variational protocol, analytic or Fock-truncated simulation (1 qubit + 1 mode, Hilbert dimension <= 512) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2608.13521 (Proves and demonstrates the separation (Thms 1-3); experiment up to k=20 against Gaussian protocols and k=40 against heterodyne/homodyne); arXiv:2609.30374 (Single squeezed mode vs classical probes for random-displacement Fourier features; information-theoretic bound plus experiment at 4.95-8.54 dB); arXiv:2402.18809 (Multimode entanglement-enabled exponential advantage for random displacement channels); arXiv:2502.07770 (Photonic experiment of 2402.18809); arXiv:2604.13177 (Qubit-oscillator computational sensing vs conventional sensing + tuned MLP; constant-factor (up to 15 points) accuracy gains); arXiv:2101.02464 (Average-case vs worst-case: why structured (average-case) families may erase exponential gaps) |
| Status | **REJECTED (shortlisted at triage, killed in adversarial review)** |
| Decision note | SHORTLISTED (replication of a known separation, Kannan Thms 1 and 3) with a new testable reality check: does the worst-case exponential gap survive structured signal families against a model-aware, tuned classical learner? The minimal test had to be rebuilt (wrong task, no energy accounting). |
| Decisive test | Fixed mean photon number E in {1, 2, 7.4 (~15 dB)} per query, k in {4, 8, 12, 16, 20}. Target: Re E[e^{ikx}] of a random single-quadrature displacement. Quantum: Kannan's depth-one ECD protocol with X readout (plus 2% readout error and ancilla dephasing), estimator = rescaled mean or prior-aware Bayes. Conventional: squeezed vacuum (sinh^2 r=E) + homodyne, with tuned learners: model-free deconvolution with CV-tuned shrinkage, oracle-prior Bayes/MLE, and an amortized MLP on homodyne histograms (grid-searched on the same folds). Families: Kannan/Baiju fringe family g(x)(1+a cos kx) (worst case) vs one narrow Gaussian vs two-component mixtures. Metric: queries N* to RMSE 0.05 on 200 held-out signals, 5 seeds, DESIGN.md section-6 paired rule on log10 N*. Pre-registered predictions: fringe family at k=16, E=1: quantum wins by >=10^2 (positive control). Single-component family: model-aware classical within 3x (predicted N_G ~ k^2/(32 E eps^2), about 2x BETTER than N_Q ~ k^2/(16 E eps^2)). The same question in its qubit-memory form is the theme's best version. |

### I-20261012-10: Entangled-probe learning of phase/frequency signals beyond classical Cramer-Rao

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch B |
| Hypothesis | Entangled-probe sensing models beat tuned classical feature extractors (MLP/RBF-SVM) at estimating and classifying noisy continuous classical signals. |
| Mechanism | GHZ/squeezed probes beat the shot-noise limit in phase estimation. |
| Data | Classical time series modulated onto a phase. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 4-qubit GHZ probe sensing a fluctuating frequency; quantum estimator vs Fourier/wavelet features + MLP; metric: MSE or accuracy at low SNR; success: lower error below shot-noise. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Decoherence washes out the gain. |
| References cited | 2608.13521 (cited for GHZ sensing) |
| Reference checks | 2608.13521 (cited for GHZ sensing): **CLAIM NOT IN PAPER** (Kannan et al. use no GHZ probes. They mention the SQL/Heisenberg-limit quadratic separation only as background and state that under Markovian noise it generically reduces to a constant factor. Their contribution is single-ancilla, entanglement-free (no multimode probe) sensing.) |
| Technical problems | The comparison is ill posed: a quantum estimator with a GHZ probe vs classical 'Fourier/wavelet features + MLP'. If the classical learner sees the raw classical time series, there is no measurement noise to beat. If it sees product-probe records, the comparison is between probe states, and post-processing cannot raise the Fisher information of the record.; For a fluctuating (stochastic) frequency, an N-qubit GHZ coherence decays as the noise characteristic function at N (superdecoherence under correlated noise). GHZ is then typically WORSE for estimating a noisy signal. Exponential gains exist only for learning high-order correlations of correlated stochastic parameters (2504.21745), which is not the stated task.; Under independent Markovian dephasing the Heisenberg gain is bounded by a constant factor (quant-ph/9707014, 1201.3940). The card lists this only as a 'risk', but it decides the outcome.; The test is quantum metrology, not machine learning; no learning separation is involved. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7, K5 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | quant-ph/9707014 (Under decoherence, GHZ and uncorrelated Ramsey give the same frequency resolution); arXiv:1201.3940 (Generic constant-factor bound on quantum enhancement with decoherence); arXiv:2504.21745 (The setting where entangled probes DO give exponential gains for stochastic signals (fine-tuned correlated phases)); arXiv:2108.06978 (ML-based adaptive phase estimation benchmarked with noisy unentangled qubits) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | REJECTED (K7, K5): GHZ Heisenberg scaling and its collapse to a constant factor under dephasing are textbook (quant-ph/9707014, 1201.3940). The citation is misattributed, the classical side's data access is undefined, and for fluctuating signals GHZ probes accumulate N-fold phase noise, so the stated advantage is false in general. |

### I-20261012-18: Single-qubit quantum sensing of sparse temporal signals

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch C |
| Hypothesis | A single-qubit feature-sensing protocol estimates sparse Fourier coefficients and temporal correlations with fewer interrogations than tuned lock-in detection, sparse Fourier regression and Bayesian estimators at matched noise and exposure. |
| Mechanism | The qubit interacts coherently with the signal; interrogation sequences extract features without estimating every component. Fair only with equal physical access and exposure. |
| Data | Classical temporal signals observed through a noisy quantum sensing interface. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | One sensor qubit, signal with 3-6 Fourier components; sweep SNR, dephasing, drift, exposure; vs lock-in, FFT, sparse regression, Bayesian design; metric: coefficient MSE vs interrogations; success: fewer interrogations at equal error, persisting under noise. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Already reported large reductions; the research is the operating envelope. |
| References cited | 2608.13521 |
| Reference checks | 2608.13521: **CLAIM NOT IN PAPER** (Partly. The paper does report large reductions with single-qubit quantum feature sensing. But its 'Fourier coefficients' are phase-space Fourier coefficients E[e^{ikx}] of a random displacement distribution, not sparse temporal-frequency components of a waveform. Its baselines are Gaussian quantum sensing protocols at equal energy (squeezed homodyne, heterodyne, two-mode squeezing), not lock-in, FFT or sparse regression. Temporal correlations (Thm 3) need a memory qubit that stays coherent across interactions.) |
| Technical problems | Conflates temporal Fourier components of a deterministic multi-tone signal (3-6 tones) with phase-space Fourier coefficients of a stochastic displacement distribution. For deterministic tones no exponential separation is known: this is spectral estimation, with at most Heisenberg-type or constant gains.; 'One sensor qubit' is the wrong architecture. Thm 1 needs a bosonic sensor plus an ancilla qubit at a fixed energy budget; a lone qubit sensor has no energy-constrained Gaussian comparison class. Thm 3 needs a memory qubit plus a fast-decohering sensor.; 'Matched noise and exposure' is undefined. It must fix the mean photon number per query, the number of signal queries and the coherence time of each side.; The success criterion ('fewer interrogations at equal error, persisting under noise') has no threshold or seed rule. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 (same source and mechanism as I-20261012-02), K4 (baselines are classical post-processing of a classical record, not the same sensor without the quantum resource) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2608.13521 (Thm 3: one memory qubit gives exponential advantage for m-point temporal correlators); arXiv:2504.21745 (Spatial (entangled-sensor) analogue for correlated stochastic parameters); arXiv:2609.38678 (Squeezing-based exponential separation for continuously evolving signals) |
| Status | **MERGED into I-20261012-02** |
| Decision note | MERGED into I-20261012-02: same paper and mechanism. Its operating-envelope sweep (SNR, dephasing, drift) and its 'equal physical access and exposure' framing are kept, but its reading of the paper (sparse temporal Fourier components, lock-in/FFT baselines) is wrong. |

### I-20261012-27: GHZ-probe phase estimation versus product probes with tuned estimators

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch D |
| Hypothesis | A GHZ-probe phase estimator beats tuned classical estimators using product probes on low-noise phase regression at equal sensing resources. |
| Mechanism | Entangled probes encode phase more sensitively; post-processing cannot recover information never acquired. |
| Data | Quantum sensor states and records under unknown phase and dephasing. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 2-10 qubits, narrow phase prior; GHZ + Bayesian/neural estimator vs product Ramsey + Bayesian, GP, MLP; matched qubit-time and shots; held-out phase MSE; success: lower MSE over a nonzero noise range. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Dephasing erases GHZ benefit; a sensing advantage, not ML. |
| References cited | 2608.13521 |
| Reference checks | 2608.13521: **CLAIM NOT IN PAPER** (No GHZ vs product-probe comparison in Kannan et al. The paper's background states the opposite caveat: under Markovian noise the Heisenberg gain reduces to a constant factor.) |
| Technical problems | The result is predetermined. Without noise, the per-shot Fisher information is N^2 (GHZ) vs N (product). Under local dephasing with optimized interrogation time the gain is bounded by a constant (quant-ph/9707014, 1201.3940). A simulation would reproduce a known formula.; 'Post-processing cannot recover information never acquired' is correct (data processing). For the same reason the choice of estimator (Bayesian, GP, MLP) cannot change the conclusion; asymptotically the Bayesian estimator already saturates the classical Fisher information.; The narrow phase prior is required because GHZ outcomes are 2*pi/N periodic. For wide priors one needs multi-N adaptive schemes, so the claim depends on the prior. 'Matched qubit-time' must also count the GHZ preparation time.; It is a sensing advantage, not ML, as the card itself notes. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | quant-ph/9707014 (GHZ vs uncorrelated Ramsey under dephasing: same resolution); arXiv:1201.3940 (Constant-factor bound with decoherence); arXiv:2108.06978 (ML estimators for phase estimation with noisy unentangled qubits); arXiv:2504.21745 (Where entanglement gives more than a constant factor: correlated stochastic signals) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | REJECTED (K7): deterministic-phase GHZ vs product probes is textbook metrology. The outcome is fixed in closed form by the (quantum) Fisher information, and tuned Bayesian/GP/MLP estimators cannot exceed it, so the test adds nothing testable. Its useful kernel (entangled vs separable sensors at matched resources) is reused in the best version, in its stochastic-signal form (2504.21745). |

### I-20261012-40: Heisenberg-limited Hamiltonian learning versus classical estimation

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | Claude |
| Hypothesis | Coherent Hamiltonian-learning protocols reach coupling error eps with total evolution time O(1/eps), while estimation from repeated short-time measurements with tuned classical ML needs O(1/eps^2). |
| Mechanism | Heisenberg-limited learning of many-body Hamiltonians (Huang, Tong, Fang, Su) uses interleaved control during evolution; the advantage is in total evolution time, not qubit count. |
| Data | Quantum: unknown spin-chain couplings. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | n=4-8; compare total evolution time vs error for the coherent protocol and for classical estimators on short-time records; success: 1/eps vs 1/eps^2 scaling visible at n<=8. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Quantum metrology rather than ML; K7. |
| References cited | Heisenberg-limited Hamiltonian learning paper: ID to verify |
| Reference checks | Heisenberg-limited Hamiltonian learning paper: ID to verify: **OK** (Real paper: arXiv:2210.03030, 'Learning many-body Hamiltonians with Heisenberg-limited scaling', Huang, Tong, Fang, Su (title and authors retrieved through the arXiv API; text read). It supports O(1/eps) total evolution time with interleaved single-qubit Clifford control and a matching Omega(1/eps) lower bound for any learner. Its eps^-2 statement concerns previous algorithms, not all control-free learners; the control-free lower bound is in 2304.07172, under conditions.) |
| Technical problems | The 1/eps^2 for 'classical estimators on short-time records' is just the Cramer-Rao bound once each experiment is capped at a fixed time t0 (Fisher information <= 4 t0^2 per experiment). That cap is an arbitrary access handicap, not a classical-vs-quantum learning separation, and estimator tuning cannot change it.; Both sides run quantum experiments. The real resource is control during evolution (2304.07172) or long coherent evolution. Recent work reaches the Heisenberg limit with static fields only (2601.10380) or with lower-bounded evolution times (2604.27838), which erodes the stated contrast.; At n=4-8, the no-control Omega(eps^-2) bound of 2304.07172 needs ETH with an extensive number of parameters and T=o(exp(N)). Small systems have resolvable discrete spectra, so a fair no-control learner using long evolution could approach Heisenberg scaling for few parameters. '1/eps vs 1/eps^2 visible at n<=8' is therefore not guaranteed for a fair baseline.; This is metrology, not ML; the card itself flags K7. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2210.03030 (HL algorithm plus matching lower bound); arXiv:2304.07172 (Proven quadratic separation with vs without quantum control); arXiv:2502.11900 (Ansatz-free HL learning with numerics); arXiv:2604.27838 (HL without short-time control); arXiv:2601.10380 (HL with static single-qubit fields; numerics); arXiv:2610.06709 (Learning under minimal (analog) access models) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | REJECTED (K7): the separation is already proven and simulated. HL many-body learning with control has a matching lower bound (2210.03030), and the control vs no-control separation T=Omega(eps^-2) is proven (2304.07172). The proposed 'classical' side (short-time records only) is an arbitrary handicap fixed by Cramer-Rao that tuned ML cannot change. At n<=8 the no-control lower bound (ETH, T=o(e^N)) is not in force, so the claimed scaling contrast would not be a fair test. |

### T2 best version: Fine-tuned vs natural stochastic signals: does the memory-qubit (or GHZ) sensing advantage survive a model-aware classical learner?

*Kind:* killing experiment. *Built from:* I-20261012-02, I-20261012-18.

- **Hypothesis:** To learn the m-point correlator c_m = E[cos(sum_j theta_j)] of a classical stochastic phase signal, a single coherent memory qubit (equivalently an m-qubit GHZ probe) needs exponentially fewer signal trajectories than any measure-then-process learner ONLY for fine-tuned (worst-case) signal families. Pre-registered prediction: for natural low-dimensional families (Gaussian AR(1) noise, random telegraph noise), the best model-aware, tuned classical learner on memoryless records is within a constant factor, while model-free classical estimators are exponentially worse. Contains a positive-control replication of a known separation (2504.21745; Kannan Thm 3).
- **Mechanism:** The memory qubit reads e^{i sum theta_j} directly: one full-contrast bit per trajectory. A memoryless per-window Ramsey bit carries only the first harmonic of theta_j, so the m-th order correlator appears in the memoryless outcome distribution with weight ~2^{-m}. Fine-tuned case: any m-1 phases are jointly uniform, so all the information sits in the m-th order term and the cost grows like ~4^m; adaptivity within a trajectory does not help. Natural low-dimensional case: c_m is a smooth function of 2 parameters that are identifiable from 1- and 2-point statistics, so a model-aware learner pays only a polynomial or constant price. Triage scratch Fisher computation (exact 2^m-outcome likelihoods, eps=0.05): ratio R = N_memoryless/N_memory = 4.3, 17, 70, 280, 1122, 4488 for m=2..7 (fine-tuned); 2-45 and non-increasing in m (AR(1)); 0.4-33 and decreasing for m>=3, memoryless WINS at m>=5 for some parameters (telegraph).
- **Data:** Synthetic classical stochastic phase signals, one fresh trajectory theta_1..theta_m per query, m in {2,...,8}. Families: F_hard (theta_1..theta_{m-1} iid U(0,2pi), theta_m = C - sum, C ~ U[-0.5,0.5]); F_ar1 (stationary Gaussian AR(1), s ~ U[0.2,0.6], rho ~ U[0.3,0.95]); F_tele (theta_j = a s_j, Markov +-1 with flip prob q, a ~ U[0.3,1.5], q ~ U[0.05,0.4]). Exact outcome distributions over 2^m bit patterns: characteristic-function expansion for Gaussian, transfer matrix for telegraph, closed form for F_hard. Sampling is exact multinomial, so cost does not grow with N. Per seed: 200 test signals, 2000 training signals for amortized learners.
- **Quantum model:** Fixed, non-variational protocol (no K3 issue): memory-qubit Ramsey with X or Y readout (2 configurations). Estimators: rescaled sample mean, and a prior-aware Bayes posterior mean (same prior as the classical side, for symmetry). Noise: dephasing gamma in {0, 0.05} per window (signal x e^{-gamma m}); 2% readout error on both sides. A PennyLane circuit (1 memory qubit, or m<=4 GHZ qubits) validates the analytic outcome model in unit tests.
- **Classical baselines:** All tuned on the same CV folds of training signals; selection never uses test signals. (1) Exact-likelihood Bayes posterior mean / MLE on a 30x30 parameter grid of the TRUE family (oracle prior; the strongest learner). (2) Amortized sklearn MLPRegressor on empirical bit-pattern frequencies: hidden {(32),(64,64),(128,128)} x alpha {1e-4,1e-2,1} x learning_rate_init {1e-3,1e-2} = 18 configurations. (3) Ridge on empirical 1- and 2-point correlators, alpha over 8 values. (4) Per-window basis pattern as a hyperparameter: all-X, alternating X/Y, random, greedy adaptive. (5) Model-free randomized-basis product estimator prod_j 2 b_j e^{i phi_j} (unbiased, variance ~4^m): reported as the naive reference, not eligible as 'best classical'. The classical side gets far more configurations than the quantum side, which satisfies DESIGN.md section 6, condition 2.
- **Access model:** Equal signal exposure: N trajectories and m windows of length Delta for both sides; equal per-window sensor (one qubit, same coupling). Quantum learner: one qubit coherent across all m windows (memory time >= m*Delta, dephasing gamma per window), prepared in |+>, accumulates the phases, one projective X/Y measurement per trajectory, arbitrary classical post-processing including the prior. Spatial reading: m sensor qubits in a GHZ state, one parity readout. Classical learner: the same qubit re-prepared and measured after EVERY window, in any local basis chosen adaptively from earlier outcomes; spatial reading: m unentangled sensors with local measurements. All m*N bits go to any classical algorithm with full knowledge of the family and its prior. This is the physically meaningful measure-then-process vs coherent-memory restriction (M8), not an arbitrary handicap. Caveat: sensor hardware is held fixed (1 bit per window); a classical instrument with higher per-window SNR would be a different hardware comparison.
- **Metric:** N*(eps=0.05): the smallest number of trajectories on a log grid 10^1..10^6 (4 points per decade) at which the held-out RMSE of the c_m estimate over 200 test signals is <= 0.05. Values above 10^6 are censored and reported with the Fisher extrapolation, flagged. R(m) = N*_classical,best / N*_memory. Per-seed slope b = least-squares slope of log10 R vs m over m=3..8. 5 seeds; each redraws the test and training signals, the noise and the CV folds.
- **Success rule:** Pre-registered. (a) Positive control, F_hard at gamma=0: memory beats the best classical learner under the DESIGN.md section-6 paired rule on log10 N* (>=4/5 seeds and mean paired difference > SD) at every m in {4,6,8}, and b >= 0.25 on 5/5 seeds (theory 0.30-0.60). If this fails, the implementation is wrong: stop. (b) Main decision, natural families at gamma=0. QUANTUM WIN ('exponential advantage survives on natural signals'): b > 0.15 on >=4/5 seeds and mean b > SD(b), for at least one of F_ar1 and F_tele. CLASSICAL WIN (prediction): b < 0.05 on >=4/5 seeds and mean b + SD(b) < 0.15, for both families. Anything else is INCONCLUSIVE. (c) The constant-factor winner at m=8 is decided separately with the section-6 rule on log10 N* and recorded as a constant or polynomial advantage, never as exponential. (d) Report whether (a) survives gamma=0.05 (predicted R ~ 4^m e^{-2 gamma m}). (e) Fairness check: if no tuned classical learner comes within 3x of its Fisher bound on a natural family, the comparison is declared unfair and fixed before any result counts.
- **Milestone:** M8 (learning from quantum-enhanced experiments); it also tests M2's average-case vs worst-case point (2101.02464) in a sensing setting.
- **Kill check:** Already done in triage, as an exact Fisher-information computation (scratch files t2_check.py and t2_tele.py): natural families give bounded ratios and the fine-tuned family gives ~4^m, so the experiment is not a foregone pure replication. Remaining check before results count: the analytic outcome model must match a PennyLane simulation for m<=4, and the tuned learners must reach within 3x of their Fisher bounds. If the model-aware learner cannot get close, the result is about learner weakness, not physics.
- **Cost estimate:** New module src/qmlrc/stochastic_sensing.py, about 300 lines: families, exact outcome distributions, multinomial sampler, memory and memoryless learners, Bayes grid, amortized MLP/ridge with GridSearchCV, N* search, Fisher reference. Tests about 120 lines: analytic vs PennyLane for m<=4, unbiasedness, Fisher values matching the triage scratch numbers. Experiment wiring in qmlrc.experiments about 60 lines. CPU: Fisher reference under 2 min. Bayes and ridge take seconds per (family, m, seed) cell; MLP about 30 s per cell, run only at m in {2,4,6,8} and gamma in {0,0.05}. Total about 60-90 single-core minutes, split into experiments of at most 10 min (one per family x seed). Optional stage 2, the bosonic Thm-1 arm of I-20261012-02 with analytic samplers: about 100 more lines and under 10 min.
- **Win teaches:** A win (exponential growth of R on a natural, low-dimensional family against the strongest model-aware learner) would be a strong M8 data point: coherent memory beating measure-then-process on signals that occur in practice. It would justify hardware-noise modelling (T2, readout) and a physical-signal follow-up such as magnetic noise spectroscopy. A constant-factor win at m=8 is recorded as a real but non-exponential sensing advantage, comparable to the ~100x of 2504.21745 Sec. 5.
- **Loss teaches:** A loss, the pre-registered expectation, would show that exponential memory or entanglement sensing advantages are tied to fine-tuned, worst-case signal distributions. On natural Gaussian or Markov signals, a model-aware classical learner on memoryless records is within a constant factor, sometimes better, and comparisons against model-free baselines overstate the advantage exponentially, a K4-type lesson. FIELD_MAP M8 should then qualify 'exponential advantage for practical classical signals' as a worst-case statement, consistent with 2101.02464.
- **Closest prior work:** arXiv:2504.21745 (Entangled vs unentangled sensors for correlated stochastic phases: ~2^N separation for the fine-tuned family; ~100x but not exponential for a classical XXZ distribution); arXiv:2608.13521 (Thm 3: one memory qubit gives O(1) vs exp(Omega(m)) for m-point temporal correlators (bosonic sensor, worst-case lower bound)); arXiv:2101.02464 (Average-case predictions need about as many runs for measure-first learners as for quantum learners); arXiv:2609.30374 (Model-free deconvolution estimator and its exponential variance: the naive-baseline mechanism in the bosonic setting); arXiv:2402.18809 (Multimode entanglement-enabled random-displacement learning (worst-case lower bound))

**Adversarial review: KILLED.** KILLED as a killing experiment. The positive-control replication is the part worth saving. (a) Already done in substance. - 2504.21745 says its exponential gap comes from the fine-tuned distribution. Its natural XXZ family gives a constant of about 100x that plateaus, and its Prop. 2 / Thm D.4 already implies the proposal's "natural families are polynomial" prediction for any low-order-identifiable family. - 2602.17591 formalizes the gap as exponential only in a "problem wedge". - 2410.05878 gives a physically motivated Gaussian family with a 2^(N-1) gap, but only when its collective-mode parameter is exponentially small. - The single-qubit memory version is already in 2504.21745 App. B and 2608.13521 Thm 3. (b) Classical can match on every fixed-dimension family. The proposal's own oracle Bayes learner closes the gap by parametric identifiability. My exact Fisher checks on zero-DC and anti-correlated Gaussian families, chosen to favour memory, still give bounded R. So the QUANTUM WIN branch cannot happen, and the natural arm is not a test. (c) Access is fair in the GHZ reading. The temporal reading must state that the memoryless side is hardware with coherence about Delta. The quantum learner is under-optimized, so R < 1 values are artifacts. (d) As specified the protocol fails. - F_hard prior SD 0.0366 < eps 0.05, so N* sits at the grid floor on both sides, R = 1, and the control orders 'stop'. - The dephasing prediction is wrong: symmetric dephasing keeps R = 4^(m-1). - One MLP grid search takes 80 s at m=8, so the MLP baseline needs about 10-40 core-hours, not 60-90 min, and breaks the 10-minute rule. - The absolute eps makes shrinking targets trivial. Salvage: re-enter F_hard alone, fixed (wide C prior, normalized metric, N grid to 10^8, hybrid quantum learner, symmetric noise), as a 'replication of a known separation'. Record the natural-family claim as a short proposition citing Prabhu Thm D.4. Any experimental natural arm should sweep a collective-mode / zero-DC parameter against m, or address Kannan's cumulant open problem. Scratch checks are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-quantum-ml-reality-check/3242e8f8-b193-546c-b381-3fe888e7979d/scratchpad/round01/t2_review/: prior_sd.py, dephase.py, mlp_time.py, zero_dc.py.

- *Already done:* Mostly yes. The decisive (natural-family) arm only confirms what published results already state. (1) Prabhu et al. 2504.21745 (full text read: abstract, Secs 1, 4.3, 6, Apps D.3-D.5): - They say outright that "this exponential advantage in sample complexity arises from the fine-tuned distribution of the parameters we choose". - Their physically motivated family (thermal classical XXZ, conserved total S^z) gives about 100x. In Fig. 5c the feature-matrix concentration plateaus, which they call "a practically large, constant advantage". - Prop. 2 / Thm D.4 gives a sufficient condition for unentangled sensors to need only polynomially many shots. That condition is met by any family whose parameters move low-Hamming-weight characteristic-function entries by Omega(1/poly), which covers the proposal's AR(1) and telegraph families. This is the discrimination version of the proposal's pre-registered prediction. - Sec. 4.3 and App. B already treat the single-qubit sequential (memory vs incoherent qubit) version and map it exactly onto GHZ vs product. - Their numerical unentangled baseline is a trained linear layer on all 2^N bit-string frequencies, essentially the proposal's amortized learner. - App. D.5: when sensing time can be optimized, the exponential gap can reduce to a Heisenberg (polynomial) advantage. (2) Kannan et al. 2608.13521 (Thm 3 and Thm E.6, the remark before E.6, App. H read): - Their memoryless lower bound uses a parity construction that they call a proof of concept, saying physically better-motivated bounds "are likely possible". - They list as open problems (i) polyspectrum/cumulant estimation and (ii) a memoryless adversary given all lower-order correlations for free. - Fig. 3(c): the memory-experiment benchmarks are simulated (Helstrom per step, squeezed homodyne), each given 5x the energy. This supports the coordinator's FIELD_MAP M8 correction. (3) Cotler, Danielson & Kannan 2602.17591 (pp. 6-7 and App. E.4 read): - The advantage factor scales as (b/eps)^2 and is exponential only in a "problem wedge" where the symmetry-breaking parameter is exponentially small. - Worst-case separations are "forced into vanishingly small corners of the instance space". - They also show parity-type exponential gaps disappear under fixed energy accounting. (4) Wang et al. 2410.05878 (full text read): - A Gaussian, physically motivated correlated-dephasing family (collective mode suppressed, e.g. conservation laws or decoherence-free subspaces) gives a 2^(N-1) per-shot QFI entanglement advantage. - It holds only for xi <~ N/2^(N-1) (their "dynamical range"), and holds regardless of prior knowledge of the nuisance correlations. (5) Wang et al. 2603.15742: - Power-law spatially correlated Gaussian noise gives only polynomial advantage, Theta(N^(1-alpha)), and O(1) for alpha > 1. (6) Hardware with natural signals: - Rosskopf et al. 1610.03253 (abstract): memory-qubit NV spectrometer, about 100x resolution gain. What the proposal adds: AR(1) and telegraph families in a temporal estimation task, scored with a tuned-learner pipeline. Its outcome is already determined by the triage's exact Fisher computation. The F_hard arm is a pure replication of 2504.21745 Sec. 4 / App. B and 2608.13521 Thm 3.
- *Classical can match:* For the natural families, yes, and provably so. The proposal's own baseline (1), exact-likelihood Bayes on the true family, already closes any exponential gap. - The reason is that c_m is a smooth function of 2 parameters, identifiable from adjacent-window statistics. Each trajectory contributes (m-1) such pairs, so model-aware Fisher information grows linearly in m while |grad c_m| = O(sqrt(m)). N_memoryless therefore stays bounded or polynomial. - This is Prabhu's Thm D.4 transposed to estimation. - I checked it numerically (exact 2^m-outcome Fisher, scratch zero_dc.py) on Gaussian families outside the proposal's prior that should favour memory most: - zero-DC increments theta_j = A_j - A_{j-1}, so Var(sum theta) stays bounded while per-window phases are large: R = 8 -> 3.4 (V=0.5), 3.3 -> 1.35 (V=2), m=3..7; - anti-correlated AR(1), rho = -0.8 and -0.95: R between 0.34 and 6 for m=3..7, oscillating rather than growing. - All fixed-dimension families give bounded R. An exponential appears only if a family parameter scales with m (xi ~ 2^-m, as in 2410.05878), which is the fine-tuning/problem-wedge regime by definition. For F_hard, no measure-then-process learner can match (Prabhu Thm D.1/D.3; Kannan Thm E.6). But as specified, the oracle-prior Bayes posterior mean matches trivially: the prior of c = cos C with C ~ U[-0.5, 0.5] has SD 0.0366 < eps = 0.05, so N* sits at the grid floor for both sides. A classical sensor with more than 1 bit per window would erase the F_hard gap too (the proposal concedes this).
- *Access model issue:* Mostly fair in the spatial reading: m-qubit GHZ vs m product sensors with local adaptive measurements is the standard, physically meaningful comparison of entangled vs separable sensors (M8). Three problems remain. (i) Temporal reading. If the sensor qubit's own T2 is at least m*Delta, coherent accumulation (Ramsey or dynamical-decoupling spectroscopy; Norris et al. 1512.01575, Sung et al. 1903.01043, abstracts read) is standard practice. Measuring the same qubit after every window is then a deliberately suboptimal protocol, not a hardware limit. The meaningful restriction is a sensor whose coherence is about Delta, either with an added memory qubit (Kannan's architecture) or without one. The proposal must say this. (ii) Asymmetry against the quantum side. The quantum learner is fixed to full-memory X/Y readout (2 configurations), while the classical side gets adaptive bases, Bayes, MLPs and ridge. The quantum learner's physical options include every memoryless strategy, since it can simply measure early. So the triage's R < 1 values ("memoryless WINS" at m >= 5, telegraph) are artifacts of a restricted quantum learner, not classical wins. This also tilts the design toward its own pre-registered "classical win". (iii) Noise asymmetry. Dephasing is applied as e^{-gamma m} to the memory signal only. Per-window sensor dephasing acts on both sides; only extra memory idle dephasing is asymmetric. Readout error (one bit vs m bits) is a legitimate physical asymmetry.
- *Feasibility:* Exact simulation at m <= 8 is easy: 256 outcomes, 3^8 characteristic-function terms, and Bayes on a 30x30 grid takes seconds to minutes. As specified, though, the protocol is not feasible or decisive. (1) The positive control fails by construction. Prior SD of c under F_hard is 0.0366 < eps = 0.05, so the prior-mean estimator already meets the RMSE target. N* sits at the grid floor (10) for both learners at every m, R = 1 and b = 0. Rule (a) then says "implementation is wrong: stop". (2) Fixing this by widening C to U[-pi, pi) pushes the classical N* above the 10^6 grid top at m >= 7: about 4^(m-1) x 400 = 1.6e6 at m=7 and 6.5e6 at m=8. That would make the decisive cells censored. Exact multinomial sampling makes a 10^8 grid free. (3) The MLP budget is wrong by 10-50x. One 18-config x 3-fold GridSearchCV on 2000x256 features (m=8) took 80 s on one core (measured, project venv, sklearn 1.9.1). Over a 21-point log-N grid that is about 28 min per (family, m, gamma, seed) cell, roughly 10-40 single-core hours in total, not 60-90 min. It breaks the 10-minute-per-experiment rule. (4) The absolute eps is not meaningful for targets that shrink with m. For AR(1) at m=8, 30% of prior draws have c < 0.05; the telegraph prior SD falls from 0.47 to 0.19. Both sides get easier with m, pushing b toward 0 regardless of physics. (5) Decisiveness. For fixed-dimension identifiable families, the QUANTUM WIN branch (b > 0.15) is mathematically impossible barring bugs. The experiment can only confirm a result the triage's exact Fisher computation already gives. A CLASSICAL WIN on two 2-parameter families cannot support the universal "ONLY fine-tuned" claim, and 2410.05878 shows a physically motivated Gaussian family with an exponential gap once its collective-mode parameter scales with N.
- *Technical errors:* Positive control is broken. F_hard uses C ~ U[-0.5, 0.5], so the prior SD of c = cos C is 0.0366 (Monte Carlo, scratch prior_sd.py), below eps = 0.05. The oracle-prior Bayes learner (baseline 1, and the memory side's Bayes) reaches RMSE <= 0.037 with N = 0, so N* = 10 on both sides and R = 1. Success rule (a) fails and the protocol orders 'stop'.; Dephasing prediction (d) 'R ~ 4^m e^{-2 gamma m}' is wrong. With the same per-window dephasing on both sides, the memoryless m-bit parity contrast also picks up e^{-gamma m}, and the optimal-readout Fisher ratio stays 4^{m-1}: exactly 64, 1024 and 16384 at m = 4, 6, 8 for gamma = 0 and 0.05 (scratch dephase.py). The e^{-2 gamma m} decay appears only if the classical sensor is noiseless (7362 at m=8). The prefactor should be 4^{m-1}, not 4^m.; Compute estimate is too low. 'MLP about 30 s per cell' contradicts a measured 80 s for a single 18x3 GridSearchCV at m=8, before multiplying by the N grid. Per-(family, seed) experiments then exceed 10 min.; Triage telegraph m=2 rank deficiency is structural, not numerical. For theta = a*s with s = +-1, cos theta_j = cos a is deterministic and E[sin theta_j] = 0. Each fixed basis pattern therefore gives at most one statistic carrying q (all-Y gives sin^2 a (1-2q)), so one pattern always yields a rank-1 Fisher matrix. Splitting trajectories across patterns fixes it.; Monotonicity claims in the mechanism are inaccurate. AR(1) at s=0.3, rho=0.5 gives R = 21.8, 40.5, 32.5, 46.5, 30.0, 33.9 (not non-increasing). Telegraph at a=2.0, q=0.1 gives 7.1, 3.5, 1.75, 2.2, 2.5 (not decreasing). That point also lies outside the proposal's prior a in [0.3, 1.5].; The triage Fisher ratios compare a model-free memory estimator with a model-aware memoryless bound, and ignore the prior. With the informative priors the experiment specifies, Bayes risks, not local Fisher bounds, set N*, so fairness check (e) ('within 3x of Fisher') can pass for the wrong reason.; The slope b is fitted over m = 3..8, but the MLP baseline runs only at m in {2,4,6,8}. 'Best classical' is therefore defined over different learner sets at odd and even m.; The hypothesis wording 'ONLY for fine-tuned families' cannot be tested with two fixed-prior 2-parameter families. 2410.05878 gives a Gaussian, physically motivated noise family with a 2^(N-1) gap once its collective-mode parameter is exponentially small, so 'natural vs fine-tuned' needs an operational definition (parameters independent of m vs scaling with m).
- *Required fixes:* Split the card. (i) Keep only F_hard as a labelled 'replication of a known separation' (2504.21745 Sec. 4 and App. B; 2608.13521 Thm 3). (ii) Record the natural-family claim as a short proposition, not an experiment: if family parameters eta in R^k are identifiable from window-pair (or <= r-point) first-harmonic statistics with per-pair Fisher information >= lambda, then N_memoryless <= |grad c_m|^2 / (lambda (m-1) eps^2), which is polynomial in m. Cite Prabhu Prop. 2 / Thm D.4 as the discrimination analogue and illustrate it with the existing exact-Fisher scripts (no MLP).; Fix the positive control. Use C ~ U[-pi, pi) (prior SD of c about 0.71) or a normalized target (RMSE <= 0.25 x prior SD of c_m). Report the prior-only RMSE as an N = 0 row in every cell, and extend the N grid to 10^8 (exact multinomial sampling makes it free) so that m = 7-8 is not censored.; Replace absolute eps by a normalized metric (RMSE divided by the prior SD of c_m, or absolute error on -log c_m), so targets that decay with m do not make the task easier for both sides.; Let the quantum learner use the best of full memory, block memory (accumulate k consecutive windows, k dividing m) and memoryless, all with the same Bayes post-processing, so that R >= 1 by construction. Report 'memoryless wins' only as 'the full-memory protocol is suboptimal'.; Apply the same per-window dephasing to both sides, and model extra memory idle dephasing gamma_mem separately. Correct prediction (d) to R ~ 4^{m-1} under symmetric noise, and R ~ 4^{m-1} e^{-2 gamma_mem m} only for idle dephasing.; Drop the amortized MLP, or amortize it across N with a single network taking log10 N as input. The oracle exact-likelihood Bayes learner is already the strongest classical learner, and at the measured 80 s per grid search the MLP breaks the 10-minute rule without changing the decision.; If an experimental 'natural' arm is kept, choose families whose outcome is not fixed by parametric identifiability, for example: (a) the 2410.05878 / problem-wedge regime, where a zero-DC or collective-mode parameter xi is swept against m to locate where the gap turns exponential (expected xi <~ 2^-m); (b) Kannan's open problem, estimating the m-th cumulant or polyspectrum of K-fluctuator telegraph noise against a memoryless learner given all lower-order correlations; (c) parametric dimension growing with m (K proportional to m fluctuators), where model-aware inversion may be ill-conditioned.; State the temporal access model precisely: the memoryless side is a sensor whose coherence time is about Delta (hardware without memory), not a long-lived qubit deliberately measured early. Otherwise use only the spatial GHZ-vs-product reading.; Give 'natural' an operational definition (family parameters fixed independently of m) and restrict the hypothesis to it. Drop the universal 'ONLY'.
- *Further prior work:* arXiv:2504.21745 (Prabhu, Kremenetski, Khan, Yanagimoto, McMahon. Full text read (abstract, Secs 1, 4.3, 6, Apps D.3-D.5). States the exponential comes from the fine-tuned distribution. XXZ gives about 100x with a plateau (Fig. 5c). Prop. 2 / Thm D.4 gives a polynomial-shot condition for unentangled sensors that covers low-order-identifiable families. Already includes the single-qubit sequential memory version. D.5: sensing-time optimization can reduce the gap to Heisenberg scaling.); arXiv:2608.13521 (Kannan, Prabhu, Khan, ..., McMahon, Cotler. Read Thm 3 / G.3, Thm E.6, the remark on the parity construction, Fig. 3 caption and App. H. Memoryless lower bound S >= 2^m (1-2delta)/eps uses a parity construction the authors call a proof of concept. The 10^7 memory comparison is against simulated benchmarks given 5x energy. Open problems: polyspectra, and a memoryless adversary given lower-order correlations.); arXiv:2602.17591 (Cotler, Danielson, Kannan. Read pp. 1, 6-7 and App. E.4. 'Problem wedge': advantage factor (b/eps)^2, exponential only when the symmetry-breaking parameter is exponentially small. Worst-case separations sit in 'vanishingly small corners'. The parity-type squeezing gap vanishes at fixed energy.); arXiv:2410.05878 (Wang, Bringewatt, Seif, Brady, Oh, Gorshkov. Full text read. Gaussian near-maximally correlated dephasing with the collective mode suppressed gives a 2^(N-1) per-shot QFI entanglement advantage, but only within the dynamical range xi <~ N/2^(N-1). Holds without prior knowledge of nuisance correlations. Closest prior work for the GHZ spatial reading on 'natural' noise.); arXiv:2603.15742 (Wang, Brady, Belliardo, Gorshkov. Read main text. Power-law spatially correlated Gaussian noise gives entanglement advantage Theta(N^(1-alpha)), log N at alpha=1, O(1) for alpha>1. Temporal 1/f^p correlations modify it. Natural Gaussian families give polynomial, not exponential, gaps.); arXiv:2412.17903 (Brady, Wang, Albert, Gorshkov, Zhuang. Abstract only: framework and limits for correlated (weak) noise estimation with entangled sensor networks.); arXiv:1610.03253 (Rosskopf, Zopes, Boss, Degen. Abstract only: NV sensing qubit plus nuclear-spin memory qubit on hardware, up to 100-fold spectral-resolution gain over no memory for natural AC and NMR signals.); arXiv:1512.01575 (Norris, Paz-Silva, Viola. Abstract only: non-Gaussian (polyspectrum) noise spectroscopy with a single coherent qubit and pulse combs, i.e. the conventional coherent-accumulation baseline.); arXiv:1903.01043 (Sung et al. Abstract only: experimental reconstruction of the leading higher-order (bispectrum) noise spectrum with a superconducting qubit.); arXiv:2610.03659 (Blue, Chin, Oros, Chuang. Abstract only: in the weak-signal limit, a sensor with persistent quantum memory beats any receiver with only classical memory between received bits, for codeword sensing. A newer memory-vs-memoryless separation.)
