## T8. Learning and classifying quantum channels and processes

### I-20261012-19: Ancilla-assisted classification of unknown quantum channels

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch C |
| Hypothesis | An entanglement-assisted quantum classifier distinguishes unseen noise channels with fewer channel uses than optimized product-probe strategies with tuned classical classifiers. |
| Mechanism | An entangled reference preserves correlations revealing how the channel acts; compare against optimized adaptive product probes. |
| Data | Quantum: unknown Pauli/depolarizing/coherent-error channels. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 1-3-qubit channel families plus reference (<=10 qubits); vs optimized product probes, shadow channel estimators, SVM/QDA/GBT on records; metric: balanced error vs channel uses; success: target accuracy with significantly fewer uses across families. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Task-dependent; small experiments cannot show exponential advantage. |
| References cited | 2206.09933 |
| Reference checks | 2206.09933: **OK** (Exists (Kardashin, Vlasova, Pervishko, Yudin, Biamonte). It studies variational parallel/sequential channel discrimination with ancillas, a VQC classifier and a kernel classifier for depolarizing channels (up to 5 qubits), against the diamond-norm bound. It does NOT compare against optimized product probes with tuned classical classifiers, and its best VQC classifier used an unentangled ansatz. Relevant prior work, but no support for the hypothesis.) |
| Technical problems | Ancilla entanglement is not generically helpful. For two unitary (coherent-error) channels, the single-use diamond norm depends only on the numerical range of U^dagger V, and system-only pure states already reach it (Toeplitz-Hausdorff), so an ancilla adds nothing. 2605.31519 shows maximally entangled probes can even be near-useless for some unitary pairs.; At 1-3 qubits every entanglement gain is a constant factor (the ancilla can raise one-shot distinguishability by at most a dimension factor). The card itself says the experiment cannot show scaling, yet its success criterion ('significantly fewer uses across families') is unanchored.; 'Unseen noise channels' gives no label definition. If the classes are channel families, full process tomography with product probes at n<=3 costs a constant, and a tuned classical classifier on it is a strong baseline the card does not pin down.; 'Shadow channel estimators' are themselves entanglement-free product-probe strategies, so listing them next to 'optimized product probes' adds nothing to the fairness of the comparison. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 (standalone), K4 (standalone: the baselines are named but no tuned fair protocol or task is fixed) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2206.09933 (Variational / kernel QML channel discrimination with ancillas, small simulations, no tuned classical baselines.); arXiv:0901.2118 (Every entangled state helps some channel-discrimination task (existence, not a learning advantage).); arXiv:2605.31519 (Entanglement can hurt channel discrimination (unitary pairs).); arXiv:2108.08488 (The Pauli-channel ancilla separation that I-36 builds on.) |
| Status | **MERGED into I-20261012-36** |
| Decision note | Its only sound core, an ancilla advantage for unknown Pauli/depolarizing channels, is the Pauli-channel separation of I-36. The rest of the family is ill posed: coherent (unitary) errors gain nothing from an ancilla in binary discrimination, and at 1-3 qubits every gain is a constant factor. |

### I-20261012-26: Entangled-probe classification of identity vs depolarizing channel

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch D |
| Hypothesis | An entangled-probe classifier beats optimised product probes at identifying an unknown depolarizing channel versus identity. |
| Mechanism | An ancilla entangled with the input makes outputs more distinguishable than any separable input; the batch states Bell-probe single-query balanced accuracy 0.875 vs at most 0.75 for product probes. |
| Data | Quantum: one-use queries to a one-qubit channel. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | Identity vs completely depolarizing on one qubit, with/without a Bell-pair ancilla; vs product probes optimised over inputs and measurements plus SVM/MLP; success: Bell probe reaches 0.875 while best product <=0.75. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Quantum-query advantage only; vanishes if the classical side gets equivalent coherent access. |
| References cited | reference unknown |
| Reference checks | reference unknown: **NOT VERIFIED** (No reference given. Recomputed here: identity vs completely depolarizing on one qubit gives Helstrom success 0.75 for any pure product probe (random search max 0.7500) and 0.875 with a Bell probe (trace norm of Phi - I/4 is 3/2). Related verified prior work: quant-ph/0103160 (Fischer et al. 2001, entanglement improves Pauli-channel estimation) and 0901.2118 (Piani & Watrous).) |
| Technical problems | Not an error but a limit: the 0.875 vs 0.75 gap is a one-shot constant. With N uses the error is 2^-N/2 (product) vs 4^-N/2 (Bell), so entanglement saves exactly a factor 2 in channel uses (log d^2 / log d). There is no scaling advantage.; 'Product probes optimised over inputs and measurements plus SVM/MLP' is meaningless for single-query data: the record is one measurement outcome, the Bayes-optimal rule is a fixed lookup, and an SVM/MLP can only reproduce it.; The success criterion ('Bell probe reaches 0.875 while best product <=0.75') is a known closed form. Simulating it tests the simulator, not a hypothesis. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 (standalone: textbook Helstrom / diamond-norm fact), K6-like: no learning content (a single-shot record is one outcome; the optimal decision is a lookup table) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | quant-ph/0103160 (Entanglement-enhanced estimation of a Pauli channel (2001).); arXiv:0901.2118 (General usefulness of entanglement for channel discrimination.); arXiv:2108.08488 (n-qubit generalisation with exponential gap for Pauli eigenvalue estimation.) |
| Status | **MERGED into I-20261012-36** |
| Decision note | Its numbers are correct (I recomputed them), but they are a closed-form textbook calculation with nothing to learn. It survives only as the n=1 calibration test of I-36's best version. |

### I-20261012-24: Optimal entanglement depth for learning quantum dynamics

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch C |
| Hypothesis | A dynamics learner using an optimised intermediate entanglement depth of training probes predicts held-out evolutions with fewer measurements than product, maximally entangled and classical-shadow baselines. |
| Mechanism | Entangled data helps or hurts depending on measurement budget (transition role of entangled data). |
| Data | Quantum probes through unknown short-time local evolutions. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 4 system + up to 4 ancilla qubits; product vs partial vs maximal entanglement; same predictor; vs shadows and tuned classical predictor; sweep budget, shot noise, dephasing; metric: held-out MSE vs measurement count. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Non-monotonic; gain may be experimental design, not intrinsic quantum advantage. |
| References cited | 2306.03481, 2112.00778 |
| Reference checks | 2306.03481: **OK** (Exists (Wang, Du, Tu, Luo, Yuan, Tao). Full partial read: it does prove the 'dual effect' (entangled data help with many measurements, can hurt with few). But its query-complexity lower bound Nm = Omega(4^n r c1 c2) grows with the Schmidt rank r, and the authors say entangled data 'may be inferior ... in terms of query complexity'. That contradicts I-24's hypothesis of fewer measurements at an intermediate depth.); 2112.00778: **OK** (Exists (Huang et al.). Its dynamics task, time-reversal-symmetry classification, uses coherent access with quantum memory (two queries, n memory qubits, Bell measurements). That mechanism differs from I-24's incoherent learning with entangled inputs, so this is general background only.) |
| Technical problems | In the cited framework the reference system is traced out and only the system is measured, so 'entangled data' act as mixed inputs on the system (the paper's Remark ii). No quantum-memory resource is involved, so there is no quantum-vs-classical separation to test.; The hypothesis (fewer measurements at an optimised intermediate entanglement depth) runs against the cited paper's own lower bound, Nm >= Omega(4^n r ...), which grows with r. Entanglement helps sample complexity N, not query complexity Nm.; 'Short-time local evolutions' predicted on held-out inputs is a regime where classical ML from product-state measurement data is already efficient on average over inputs (2210.14894; average-case statement of 2101.02464 in FIELD_MAP M2). A win would not be a quantum advantage.; The 'classical-shadow baseline' is just another incoherent POVM learner, already covered by Wang et al.'s Theorem 2 (l-outcome POVMs). |
| Kill-criteria check | K7, K4 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2306.03481 (Same mechanism, theory and numerics at n=4, 6, 8.); arXiv:2007.04900 (NFL with entangled data sets, tested on Rigetti hardware.); arXiv:2210.14894 (Classical ML predicts outputs of arbitrary processes efficiently on average.); arXiv:2112.00778 (Memory-assisted coherent learning of dynamics on hardware (different mechanism).) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | K7: Wang et al. (2306.03481) already proved the transition and ran this exact sweep (n=4, Schmidt rank 1..16, m=10..20000; also n=6, 8). K4: there is no defined tuned classical baseline, and both arms are measure-first learners, so the test asks about input-state design, not quantum vs classical. The cited theory even predicts that more entanglement does not reduce total measurements. |

### I-20261012-36: Entangled-ancilla Pauli-channel learning versus ancilla-free strategies

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | Claude |
| Hypothesis | Estimating the Pauli eigenvalues of an unknown n-qubit Pauli channel with an entangled ancilla needs exponentially fewer channel uses than any ancilla-free strategy; measure the crossover at n=1..4. |
| Mechanism | Bell-state input plus Bell measurement reveals all Pauli eigenvalues at once; known lower bounds for ancilla-free strategies; information lost at measurement cannot be recovered classically. |
| Data | Quantum: queries to unknown Pauli channels. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | n=1..4 with n ancillas (<=8 qubits); random sparse Pauli channels; ancilla-assisted vs best ancilla-free adaptive strategies with tuned estimators; channel uses to reach eps; success: crossover with growing gap. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Known theorem (K7); small-n gap small. |
| References cited | Pauli-channel estimation separation paper: ID to verify |
| Reference checks | Pauli-channel estimation separation paper: ID to verify: **OK** (Resolved to 2108.08488 (Chen, Zhou, Seif, Jiang, 'Quantum advantages for Pauli channel estimation'): ancilla-assisted O(n/eps^2) vs ancilla-free Omega(2^{n/3}), even adaptive and concatenating. Also 2309.13461 (Chen, Oh, Zhou, Huang, Jiang, 'Tight bounds on Pauli channel learning without entanglement'): Theta(2^n/eps^2) vs Theta(1/eps^2). Also 2309.14326 (Chen & Gong): zero-ancilla Omega(2^n/eps^2), and O(log n) ancillas suffice with concatenation. Title and authors retrieved; relevant pages read.) |
| Technical problems | Wrong channel family. The minimal test uses 'random sparse Pauli channels', but the lower bounds use dense hard instances (perturbed completely depolarizing channels, all 4^n error rates ~4^-n). For s-sparse channels an unentangled learner gets the error rates to l_inf eps' with O(log(n/eps')/eps'^2) uses (2105.02885). Thresholding at 2eps' gives l1 error <= 3 s eps', so every eigenvalue is within 3 s eps'. Eigenvalue error eps then costs O(s^2 log(ns/eps)/eps^2) uses: polynomial, with no exponential gap to measure.; No 'crossover' exists in the ideal model. With the best known non-adaptive entanglement-free design (MUB stabilizer covering), the ancilla side is ahead at every n >= 1 by about 2^n+1. Recomputed for the revealed-a hypothesis test at 0.9 success: ratios 3.0, 5.1, 8.9, 17.0, 33.8 for n=1..5. A crossover appears only when the ancilla side has noisy Bell pairs or memory (2309.13461 Fig. 2; 2408.03376), so Bell-pair fidelity is what must be swept.; 'Any ancilla-free strategy' cannot be simulated. At n<=5 the proven entanglement-free lower bound (N >= 0.01 (4^n-1)/(2^n eps^2), about 2^n at eps=0.1) is below the ancilla-assisted Hoeffding cost (~360 at eps=0.1, delta=1/3). A small-n run can therefore only compare against the best known entanglement-free designs and estimators, never certify optimality.; Correct: a Bell input plus Bell measurement samples the Pauli error distribution, and (-1)^<c,b> is an unbiased +-1 estimator of every eigenvalue lambda_b at once. The mechanism statement is right. |
| Kill-criteria check | K7 (partially: the separation is a proven theorem and was demonstrated on 64-qubit hardware; kept only as a replication of a known separation with new structured-noise and noisy-memory arms) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2108.08488 (Theorem: n-qubit ancilla O(n/eps^2) vs ancilla-free Omega(2^{n/3}); k-ancilla trade-off Theta(n 2^{n-k}) non-adaptive.); arXiv:2309.13461 (Tight entanglement-free Theta(2^n/eps^2) (incl. concatenation, mid-circuit feedforward); noisy-Bell-pair analysis.); arXiv:2408.03376 (Hardware demonstration up to 64 qubits (hypothesis test) and 16 qubits (real noise); no structure-exploiting entanglement-free baseline.); arXiv:2309.14326 (Zero-ancilla Omega(2^n/eps^2) even concatenating; log n ancillas suffice with concatenation.); arXiv:2606.04096 (Tuned ancilla-free MLE for 2-local sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise: per-parameter accuracy ~independent of n; the natural strong classical baseline.); arXiv:2105.02885 (Ancilla-free error-rate learning in l_inf with O(log(n/eps)/eps^2) uses: kills the sparse-channel version.) |
| Status | **SHORTLISTED (weakened in adversarial review: apply the required fixes before building)** |
| Decision note | This is the only M8-legitimate, fairly posed mechanism in the theme. The quantum-memory vs measure-then-process restriction is the physically meaningful one. The separation is proven (2108.08488, 2309.13461) and observed on hardware (2408.03376). What is new and testable is whether it survives against a tuned entanglement-free learner that exploits realistic local (sparse Pauli-Lindblad) noise structure, and where noisy memory erases it. The card's test must be corrected first (wrong channel family; no 'crossover' in the ideal model). |
| Decisive test | Pauli-eigenvalue learning, n = 1..5 system qubits (+ n ancillas, <= 10 qubits), same N channel uses for both sides. Ancilla-assisted Bell sampling vs the best tuned entanglement-free design and estimator, on: (H) the dense hard family; (S) 2-local ring sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise; (M) S plus an unmodelled weight-n correlated term. Decisive quantity: the slope of log2[N*_EF/N*_EA] vs n over n = 2..5. On H the theory predicts ~1. On S it is the open question: <=0.3 means only a constant-factor gain on realistic noise, >=0.7 means the gain persists against structure-exploiting learners. A 30-minute pilot at n=4 (correct model, crude NNLS estimators) already showed a ~4-5x l_inf gap, i.e. ~20x in samples, so the outcome is not foregone. |

### T8 best version: Ancilla-assisted vs entanglement-free Pauli-channel learning (n=1-5): replication on hard channels, stress test on structured, misspecified and noisy-memory settings

*Kind:* replication of a known separation. *Built from:* I-20261012-36, I-20261012-19, I-20261012-26.

- **Hypothesis:** At equal channel uses, Bell-sampling learning with an n-qubit ancilla estimates Pauli eigenvalues about (2^n+1)x more sample-efficiently than the best known entanglement-free design on the dense hard family. Open question tested: on 2-local sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise, against a tuned entanglement-free learner that fits the local model, does the sample ratio N*_EF/N*_EA keep growing with n (slope >= 0.7 in log2) or saturate to a constant (slope <= 0.3)? Under an unmodelled correlated term, does model-free Bell sampling beat every tuned entanglement-free learner beyond a computable budget?
- **Mechanism:** Bell input + Bell measurement turns each channel use into one sample c of the Pauli error distribution p. Every eigenvalue lambda_b = E[(-1)^<c,b>] is then estimated from every sample, because all P_b (x) P_b^T commute. Without system-ancilla entanglement, one setting reveals at most a 2^n-element commuting (stabilizer) set of eigenvalues, so a given high-weight eigenvalue is informed by only ~1/(2^n+1) of the shots (Theta(2^n/eps^2) is tight, 2309.13461). Structure is the escape route for the classical side: if noise has O(n) local generators, all eigenvalues follow from local ones that every product setting measures in parallel. The entanglement-free cost per parameter may then become n-independent (2606.04096), and only constants separate the two sides.
- **Data:** Synthetic Pauli channels, 5 seeds; each seed redraws 20 tuning instances, 50 test instances and all measurement randomness. (H) Le Cam family: completely depolarizing vs lambda_a = +-1/3 for a uniformly random a, revealed after measurement (as in 2408.03376). (D) dense random Pauli channels (Dirichlet error rates). (S) 1D-ring 2-local sparse Pauli-Lindblad channels: all weight-1 and nearest-neighbour weight-2 generators, rates U(0, 0.02). (M) S plus one unmodelled Z^{(x)n} generator, rate rho in {0.003, 0.01}. Secondary: (H, S) with noisy Bell pairs (depolarizing, F in {1, 0.99, 0.97, 0.95}) and matched single-qubit SPAM noise for the entanglement-free side. Optional: a k-ancilla sweep k = 0..n (Algorithm 1 of 2108.08488).
- **Quantum model:** n system + n ancilla qubits (n <= 5, so <= 10 qubits): n Bell pairs, one use of the unknown channel on the system half, Bell measurement, which yields one Pauli-error sample per use. Estimators (tuned on training instances): empirical Walsh-Hadamard eigenvalues (model-free); NNLS/WLS fit of a sparse Pauli-Lindblad model with generator-weight cutoff w in {1, 2-ring, 2-all, 3, n}; exact MLE of that model (4^n-outcome likelihood, JAX + L-BFGS). The production sampler draws from the exactly computed Choi-diagonal distribution. It is validated against PennyLane default.mixed density-matrix circuits for n <= 2 (and a 10-qubit statevector check at n = 5) to total variation < 1e-9.
- **Classical baselines:** Entanglement-free learner (measure-then-process; adaptivity and concatenation permitted by the access model, implemented designs non-adaptive). Designs: random Pauli bases; cyclic 3^n Pauli bases; the (2^n+1)-setting MUB stabilizer covering (optimal non-adaptive design, 2108.08488 Cor. 2; GF(2^n) construction); the 9-setting local design for 2-local noise. Estimators: empirical Pauli fidelities; NNLS/WLS sparse Pauli-Lindblad fits with the same weight cutoffs; exact MLE (2606.04096-style, computed exactly at n <= 5); a thresholded population-recovery / sparse Walsh estimator (2105.02885-style). Design x estimator x cutoff x regularisation (5 values) are chosen by cross-validation on training instances, on the same shot folds as the quantum side. The quantum side never gets a larger grid than the classical side (DESIGN.md §6 rule 2). For H, both sides also get the Bayes-optimal likelihood-ratio test after a is revealed (the strongest possible processing of their data).
- **Access model:** Both sides: the same unknown n-qubit Pauli channel; the same budget N counted in channel uses; noiseless preparation and measurement in the main arm (matched noise in the secondary arm); unlimited classical memory and compute; the same tuning instances and shot folds. Quantum (entanglement-assisted): an n-qubit ancilla held coherently across exactly one channel use, Bell-pair preparation and Bell measurement; non-adaptive; no concatenation. Classical (entanglement-free): any state preparation and measurement on the n system qubits, including stabilizer states entangled within the system; adaptive control, concatenation and mid-circuit measurement with feedforward are allowed by the model (2309.13461 Prop. 1 makes this equivalent to separable system-ancilla operations), but no system-ancilla entanglement. The advantage exists only because the classical side lacks quantum memory. That restriction (measure-then-process vs coherent processing) is the physically meaningful M8 one, not an arbitrary handicap. Limitation stated up front: we simulate only the best known entanglement-free designs, not all adaptive strategies, and at n <= 5 the proven lower bound cannot certify optimality.
- **Metric:** Primary: N*, the smallest budget on a 25-point log grid (10^2 to 10^6) at which the median, over 50 test instances, of the l_inf error over all 4^n - 1 eigenvalues is <= 0.05. For H, N* is the smallest budget with revealed-a test success >= 0.9. Ratio R(n) = N*_EF,best / N*_EA,best; slope s = least-squares slope of log2 R(n) over n = 2..5. Secondary: per-seed l_inf error at fixed budgets N in {10^3, 10^4, 10^5}, compared with the DESIGN.md §6 paired rule; the bias floor and N_bias for M; the crossover Bell fidelity F*(n) where R(n) < 1.
- **Success rule:** Pre-registered. (0) Calibration gate, or the run is void: the n=1 identity-vs-depolarizing Helstrom values 0.875 / 0.75 are reproduced; samplers match exact density-matrix outcome distributions; on H the MUB ratio R(n) is within 15% of 2^n+1 for n = 1..5. (1) Replication (H): EA beats the best tuned EF under the §6 paired rule (better on >= 4/5 seeds and mean paired difference > SD) at every n and budget where EA success >= 0.75, and s_H is in [0.8, 1.2]. (2) Structured (S, correct model): s_S <= 0.3 is recorded as 'no exponential separation against structure-exploiting EF learners; constant gain C = mean R'. s_S >= 0.7 is recorded as 'separation persists against structured learners at n <= 5'. In between, inconclusive. (3) Misspecified (M): EA model-free beats the best tuned EF (CV model selection up to weight n allowed) under §6 at all budgets >= N_bias(rho, n). If no such budget exists within 10^6, the claim of model-free value fails. (4) Noisy memory: report F*(n) and compare it with the prediction from 2309.13461's estimator-variance formula (variance inflated by (1-p)^{-4|b|}).
- **Milestone:** M8 (learning from quantum experiments with quantum memory); the noisy-Bell-pair arm also informs M9
- **Kill check:** Cheapest first step (about 1 hour): implement the samplers plus NNLS only, run S and M at n = 2..5 with crude estimators, and check the H calibration. If the H ratio departs from 2^n+1, there is a bug; stop and fix. If even the crudest EF learner already gives R(n) flat and <= 2 on S, the structured question is settled without the tuned grid: the result is a constant-factor gain, and only the replication and misspecification arms remain worth running. If tuned EF with model selection matches EA on M at every budget, the 'model-free value' angle dies.
- **Cost estimate:** ~800 new lines. src/qmlrc/pauli_channels.py (~300: symplectic Pauli algebra, fast Walsh-Hadamard transform, dense / sparse Pauli-Lindblad / Le Cam channel generators, EA and EF samplers, MUB stabilizer covering via GF(2^n)); src/qmlrc/channel_estimators.py (~250: empirical, NNLS/WLS, exact JAX MLE, thresholded population recovery); two experiments registered in qmlrc.experiments (~120: replication H+D; stress test S+M+noise); tests (~150, incl. PennyLane default.mixed cross-checks and the Helstrom unit test). CPU: the scratch pilot (n=4, 30 configurations, unvectorised loops) took 5 s, and the H-ratio calculation for n=1..5 took 0.6 s. Full grid estimated at 3-8 single-core minutes per experiment; exact MLE at n=5 dominates, so restrict it to budgets near N* to stay under 10 minutes.
- **Win teaches:** If s_S >= 0.7, quantum memory keeps a growing sample advantage even when the classical learner knows the noise is local. That is direct evidence that entanglement-enhanced noise characterisation is useful on realistic hardware noise, beyond adversarial channels, and it would raise FIELD_MAP M8's 'scientific usefulness' claim. A clear N_bias on M quantifies when model-free ancilla-assisted learning beats model-based classical learning (detecting unmodelled correlated errors), and F*(n) gives a hardware target for Bell-pair fidelity.
- **Loss teaches:** If s_S <= 0.3, the exponential separation belongs to unstructured or adversarial channels. For local noise, the realistic case, the ancilla buys only a measured constant factor C, so its practical value reduces to robustness to model misspecification (arm M) or must be bought with high-fidelity memory (F*). That gives FIELD_MAP M8 a precise, quotable boundary on the most credible QML-advantage direction. Failure of the replication arm would indicate an implementation error, since H is a theorem.
- **Closest prior work:** arXiv:2108.08488 (Theorem and k-ancilla trade-off; source of the MUB design and hard family.); arXiv:2309.13461 (Tight entanglement-free bound; noisy-Bell-pair estimator used for the F sweep.); arXiv:2408.03376 (Hardware replication at 4-64 qubits; no structure-exploiting EF baseline, which is the gap this test fills.); arXiv:2606.04096 (Strong ancilla-free MLE baseline for 2-local sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise; no EA comparison.); arXiv:2105.02885 (Ancilla-free efficient error-rate learning; shows sparse channels carry no exponential gap.); arXiv:2309.14326 (Zero-ancilla tight bound; log n ancillas suffice with concatenation (optional arm).); arXiv:2201.09866 (Sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise model used for the structured family.)

**Adversarial review: WEAKENED.** WEAKENED. Arm H (dense hard family) is a correct but tautological replication: R = 2^n+1 holds by construction, and the separation is proven (2108.08488, 2309.13461) and shown on hardware (2408.03376). The proposal's 'open question' on structured local noise is essentially settled by theory. 2606.20535 proves ancilla-free learning of local Lindbladians is optimal up to log n even against adaptive learners with ancillas and entangled measurements; 2606.04096 shows n-independent entanglement-free per-parameter cost. My Cramér–Rao calculation on the proposal's own S family and metric gives R(n) ≈ 13.8-13.9 for n = 3..6, i.e. a constant of about 14 (6.9 at the non-comparable n = 2). Several pre-registered rules misfire: - The H calibration gate fails at n = 3 (−24%) for a correct implementation, because N*_EA = 74 lies below the grid minimum of 100. - The S slope over n = 2..5 is 0.303, on the inconclusive boundary, driven by n = 2. - The M-arm bias floors (about 0.007-0.056) are mostly below the 0.05 threshold, so N* cannot see the misspecification. CV selection up to weight n would also include the true generator, collapsing M onto S. - The F sweep cannot produce F*: on H a crossover needs F ≈ 0.7, and with matched SPAM on S none exists. - The cost estimate ignores about 10^7 tuning fits. The main access model, quantum memory versus measure-then-process, is fair and is the meaningful M8 restriction. The noisy arm's 'matched SPAM' is undefined and decides the outcome. To survive: - Fix the grid. - Pre-register the Fisher prediction (about 14) and test agreement and saturation on n = 3..5. - Turn M into an eps-scaling test of Bell sampling (eps^-2) against the best ancilla-free sparse non-local learners (eps^-4, 2607.23044); that is a point 2606.20535 leaves open. - Use a gate-level, knowledge-symmetric noise model with F down to 0.6. Scratch scripts are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-quantum-ml-reality-check/3242e8f8-b193-546c-b381-3fe888e7979d/scratchpad/round01/t8_review/: fisher_ratio.py, misspec_bias.py, noisy_fisher.py, hgrid.py, dirichlet_trivial.py. Reading log: - Selected full-text pages via alphaXiv: 2408.03376, 2606.04096, 2309.13461, 2108.08488, 2602.21510, 2606.20535. - Abstracts only: 2607.23044, 2606.23652, 2606.20706, 2607.19017. - Titles/authors only via the arXiv API: the remaining IDs. - Tool use: one arxiv_search.py call and two id_list curls. No repository files were modified.

- *Already done:* Mostly done, or answered in theory. (1) Arm H, the dense hard family: the separation is proven. 2108.08488 gives O(n/eps^2) for entanglement-assisted learning and a tight non-adaptive bound; its Cor. 2 uses a (2^n+1)-element MUB stabilizer covering, which the paper states is optimal for non-adaptive, non-concatenating protocols. 2309.13461 proves Theta(2^n/eps^2) per eigenvalue without entanglement, and its Prop. 1 shows separable schemes are equivalent to mid-circuit measurement with feedforward. 2408.03376 demonstrated the separation on IBM hardware: the same Le Cam test at n = 4..32 (up to 64 qubits per the abstract), and gate-layer noise learning up to 16 qubits with a fitted overhead of 1.33^w against the 2^w entanglement-free lower bound. In simulation, arm H can only reproduce R = 2^n+1, which holds by construction. It is a calibration, not new evidence. (2) Arm S, the 'open question', is essentially answered. 2606.20535 (Arad et al.) learns local Lindbladians without ancillas, using product states and Pauli measurements, with O~(Lambda^2/eps^2 log n) channel uses. Its matching Omega(Lambda^2/eps^2) lower bound holds even for adaptive learners with arbitrary ancillas and entangled measurements. So for local noise, quantum memory can gain at most log n times constants. 2606.04096 (Belkin et al.) shows in simulation (n = 5..30, 9 settings plus MLE) that the entanglement-free per-parameter MSE does not depend on n. Footnote 44 of 2108.08488 already notes that entangled strategies have no large advantage for error rates in l_inf (citing 2105.02885). What remains unpublished, as far as I found, is the numerical constant N*_EF/N*_EA for sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise. A Cramér–Rao calculation gives it in seconds: about 14 (below). (3) The noisy-memory arm repeats 2309.13461 Fig. 2 and S5, and 2408.03376 (error-mitigated EEL, EMEEL). What the proposal adds: a small-scale check, with tuned baselines, of a constant the theory predicts. That passes K7 only as a 'replication / confirmation of a predicted constant', not as an open question.
- *Classical can match:* The entanglement-free side cannot remove the constant gap on S. But the proposal overlooks tools that settle its main question without the experiment, plus baselines that would shrink the gap. (a) The asymptotic Cramér–Rao/Fisher calculation (the framework of 2602.21510: MLE sample complexity is set by the inverse Fisher information). My script (scratchpad round01/t8_review/fisher_ratio.py) evaluates the proposal's own metric, the l_inf error over all 4^n-1 eigenvalues. It compares Bell sampling with the equal-allocation 9-setting design on the S family (rates U(0.001, 0.02)). R(n) = 6.9, 13.8, 13.9, 13.9 and 13.7 for n = 2..6; the ratio for per-rate MSE is about 14 from n = 3 on. Total run time is about 7 s. The answer to 'grows or saturates?' is therefore 'saturates at about 14 from n = 3', before any sampling. (b) Better entanglement-free designs: allocation-optimised or D-optimal choices over product settings, and random-product shadow process tomography (as in 2606.20535). These can only lower R below 14. (c) Cycling/concatenation (2606.04096 §V.A). Essential in the SPAM arm. (d) Bayes posterior-mean estimators using the known prior U(0, 0.02): the tuning instances come from that prior, so this is fair to both sides. (e) For arm M, ansatz-free entanglement-free learners: population recovery (2105.02885), and product-measurement learners of sparse non-local Lindbladians (2607.23044, O~(Gamma^2 M0^2/eps^4) experiments; 2606.23652). A Z^{(x)n} generator flips all bits in the 9-setting (X,X) setting with probability about rho, where local noise does so with probability about rho_loc^{n/2}. It is easy to detect without memory.
- *Access model issue:* Main arm: fair, and it is the physically meaningful M8 restriction: no system-ancilla quantum memory, i.e. measure-then-process. Both sides get the same channel uses and noiseless state preparation and measurement (SPAM), and the entanglement-free side may use stabilizer states entangled within the system. On H the advantage exists only because the entanglement-free side lacks memory, which is the legitimate 'learning from experiments' separation. Problems are in the secondary arm. (i) 'Matched single-qubit SPAM noise' is undefined, and the result depends entirely on that convention. With per-qubit depolarizing p on prep and measurement, the entanglement-free eigenvalues attenuate by (1-p)^{2|b|}, the same factor as the Bell-pair convention of 2309.13461 (noise once on each of 2n qubits). With that matching, R on S stays at 12-14 for every F in {0.90..1}, and no crossover exists. With a noiseless entanglement-free side, R is still 7.6 at F = 0.95 and 4.3-4.6 at F = 0.90. On H, a crossover needs (2^n+1)(1-p)^{4|a|} ≈ 1, i.e. F ≈ 0.7. So F*(n) does not exist inside the proposed sweep {1, 0.99, 0.97, 0.95}. (ii) Who knows the noise must be symmetric. 2408.03376 pays extra counted uses (auxiliary entanglement-free learning, AuxEFL) to learn the ancilla noise. Giving the entanglement-assisted side its p for free, while the entanglement-free side gets no SPAM knowledge, would be an arbitrary handicap. (iii) The entanglement-free side must be allowed to concatenate and fit decays, the standard SPAM-robust method. The proposal permits it in the access model but implements only designs without concatenation. (iv) Bell preparation and measurement need CNOTs, so a gate-level noise model, not 'fidelity F', is the fair way to match the two sides.
- *Feasibility:* Simulation is trivial. The entanglement-assisted sampler is a 4^n-outcome distribution (1024 at n = 5), and the Fisher calculation for n = 2..6 ran in about 7 s. The 10-qubit cap holds for n ≤ 5. n = 6 would need 12 qubits on the entanglement-assisted side, so the structured trend rests on only three comparable points (n = 3, 4, 5). The CPU estimate is not credible as written. Design (4) × estimator (4) × cutoff (5) × regularisation (5) is about 400 entanglement-free configurations. Multiplied by 20 tuning instances × 25 budgets × 5 seeds × 4 families × n = 1..5, that is about 10^7 fits, many of them exact MLE. The 5 s pilot excluded tuning, and the '3-8 min' figure ignores this multiplicity. Decisiveness is poor because several pre-registered rules misfire. (1) The H calibration gate voids a correct run. (2) The S slope rule lands on its threshold. (3) The M arm's primary metric cannot see the misspecification. (4) The F sweep cannot produce F*. Details are under technical_errors. Even if fixed, the S outcome is predictable: about 14, flat.
- *Technical errors:* H calibration gate is unachievable on the stated budget grid. The true N*_EA for the revealed-a test (success >= 0.9, lambda_a = ±1/3) is 74 at every n, below the grid minimum of 10^2, so it is censored at 100; the grid step is 1.468x. On the proposal's grid, R(n) = 3.16, 4.64, 6.81, 14.68, 31.62 for n = 1..5. At n = 3 that is −24% from 2^n+1 = 9, so gate (0), 'within 15%', voids a correct implementation (scratchpad t8_review/hgrid.py).; S-arm slope rule is ill-posed. The asymptotic Cramér–Rao ratio is R = 6.9, 13.8, 13.9, 13.9 for n = 2..5 (13.7 at n = 6), giving s = 0.303 over n = 2..5: on the 0.3 boundary, so likely 'inconclusive' for a ratio that is flat from n = 3 on. The n = 2 point is not comparable: a 2-site 'ring' has one bond, so its 15 generators are the complete 2-qubit model, whereas rings with n ≥ 3 have n bonds.; At n ≤ 5, 'exponential' and 'constant' cannot be told apart by magnitude: the structured constant (about 14) exceeds 2^n+1 at n = 2 and 3. A 1.33^w-type growth (2408.03376) would give slope 0.41, which falls in the inconclusive band.; The M arm's primary metric is blind to the unmodelled term. The bias floor of the best-fitting local model (exact KL projection, scratchpad misspec_bias.py) is: rho = 0.003, about 0.007 (entanglement-assisted) and 0.007-0.017 (entanglement-free); rho = 0.01, about 0.023 and 0.023-0.056. Most of these are below the 0.05 threshold that defines N*. N_bias is never defined. 'CV model selection up to weight n' (combinatorial, unspecified) includes Z^{(x)n} as a candidate, which makes the tuned entanglement-free learner consistent and collapses M onto S.; Noisy-memory arm: no crossover F*(n) exists in the sweep F ∈ {0.95..1}. On S, R ≥ 7.5 with a noiseless entanglement-free side and 12-14 with matched SPAM. On H, the crossover needs F ≈ 0.7. The 'hardware target F*' output would be empty, or an artefact of the matching convention.; The cost estimate omits the cross-validation multiplicity (about 10^7 fits; see feasibility).; D family: the Dirichlet concentration is unspecified. The eigenvalues shrink like 2^-n: the all-zero estimator already has median l_inf error 0.10 at n = 5 (alpha = 1), against the fixed threshold 0.05. A CV-tuned shrinkage estimator on the entanglement-free side then compresses R below 2^n+1 at the largest n, so the family's difficulty changes with n.; Minor: Theta(2^n/eps^2) in 2309.13461 is per eigenvalue at success 2/3. The proposal's simultaneous l_inf over all 4^n−1 eigenvalues adds a union-bound factor of about n on both sides; harmless for the ratio, but the cited bound is quoted for a different task.
- *Required fixes:* Fix the H gate. Start the budget grid at about 10 and get N* by bisection or interpolation, or compute H success analytically (it is a binomial test). Set the tolerance to match grid resolution. Treat H as a unit test, not evidence.; Re-label arm S as 'confirmation of a theoretically predicted constant' (2606.20535, 2606.04096), not an open question. Pre-register the Cramér–Rao prediction R∞ ≈ 14 (equal-allocation 9-setting design, l_inf over all eigenvalues; scratchpad t8_review/fisher_ratio.py). Test (a) whether the finite-sample tuned N* ratio at n = 3, 4, 5 agrees with it within ±30%, and (b) whether an optimised entanglement-free design (allocation-optimised or D-optimal) lowers it. Replace the n = 2..5 slope with a pre-registered saturation test on n = 3..5, e.g. R(5)/R(3) ≤ 1.5 means constant; ≥ 2.5 means growing.; Redesign M so it targets a genuinely open point. 2606.20535 leaves open whether ancilla-free learners reach the standard quantum limit for M-sparse non-local generators; the best known product-measurement learner scales as eps^-4 (2607.23044), against eps^-2 for Bell sampling. Measure N*(eps) for eps ∈ {0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005}, at ε values below the bias floor. Fit the eps-scaling exponent for Bell sampling and for the best ancilla-free learners: population recovery (2105.02885) and sparse non-local Lindblad fits with an explicitly defined candidate generator set. An exponent gap at fixed n ≤ 5 is a decisive small-scale test; the n-slope is not.; Noisy arm. Use a gate-level noise model for both circuits: Bell preparation = H + CNOT per pair, an idle memory error while the channel acts, and single-qubit rotations for entanglement-free preparation. Make knowledge symmetric: both sides know their SPAM/memory noise, or both pay counted uses to learn it (as AuxEFL does in 2408.03376). Allow the entanglement-free side to concatenate and fit decays. Sweep F down to about 0.6 so that a crossover can exist (H predicts F* ≈ 0.7).; Prune the tuning grid and recompute the cost. MLE is asymptotically efficient, so on S use MLE with the correct model and tune only at three budgets near N*. Fix the generator cutoff per family. Report configuration counts on both sides, per DESIGN.md §6.; Specify the Dirichlet concentration and scale the tolerance to the typical |lambda_b|, or drop family D (H already carries the replication).; Report the analytic Fisher/Cramér–Rao ratio next to every Monte Carlo N* ratio, as the reference that decides whether the simulation adds anything beyond the asymptotics.
- *Further prior work:* arXiv:2606.20535 (Arad, Chen, Guo, Rebentrost, Yu, 'Near-Optimal Learning of Local Lindbladians'. Read selected full-text pages: abstract, results, related work, Thm 3.11 and lower-bound section. Ancilla-free, non-adaptive, product-state/Pauli-measurement algorithm with O~(Lambda^2/eps^2 log n) channel uses. Matching Omega(Lambda^2/eps^2) lower bound for adaptive learners with arbitrary ancillas and entangled measurements: neither entanglement nor adaptivity helps beyond log factors for local noise. This answers most of arm S. It flags as open whether ancilla-free learners reach the standard quantum limit for M-sparse non-local Lindbladians, which is the better target for arm M.); arXiv:2602.21510 (Kwon, Lie, Jiang, 'Universal Sample Complexity Bounds in Quantum Learning Theory via Fisher Information Matrix'. Read selected full-text pages: MLE sample complexity for l_inf is set by max diag of the inverse Fisher information, up to a log factor; applied to Pauli channel learning with and without entanglement. Justifies computing R(n) analytically instead of by simulation.); arXiv:2607.23044 (Zhou, Gong, 'Characterizing Arbitrary Lindbladian Dynamics with a Few Pauli Measurements'. Abstract only: sparse Lindbladians with no locality assumption, learned from product Pauli preparations and measurements in O~(Gamma^2 M0^2/eps^4) experiments, supports identified from data. A strong ancilla-free baseline for arm M; the eps^-4 vs eps^-2 gap is the testable point.); arXiv:2606.23652 (Möbus, Bergamaschi, Stilck França, Rouzé, 'Robust Structure Learning of k-local Lindbladians'. Abstract only: product states, short-time evolution and single-qubit Pauli measurements suffice for k-local generators without knowing the structure. Further ancilla-free evidence for arm S.); arXiv:2606.20706 (Sinha, 'Efficient and SPAM-Robust Ansatz-Free Lindbladian Learning'. Abstract only: Bell-sampling ansatz-free learner with SPAM-gauge characterisation. Relevant to a symmetric SPAM treatment in the noisy arm.); arXiv:2607.19017 (Kang, Jeon, Oh, 'Bound Entanglement Is Insufficient for an Exponential Quantum Learning Advantage'. Abstract only: Pauli-channel learning with inputs or measurements obeying the reduction criterion has no exponential advantage. Context for which memory resources matter (noisy-Bell-pair arm).); arXiv:2606.18188 (Romanov, Ivashkov, Gong, Kannan, Gu, Hu et al., 'Learning Arbitrary Lindbladians with Quantum Error Correction'. Title/authors via arXiv API, plus the summary in 2606.20535's related work (not read directly): ancillas + Clifford control reach the standard quantum limit for ansatz-free (unstructured) learning. Shows that ancillas matter for unstructured, not local, noise.); arXiv:2606.30358 (Lewis, Tang, Wright, 'Learning the structure of open quantum systems'. Title/authors via arXiv API; content known only from 2606.20535's description (constant-local Lindbladians, ancilla-free). NOT READ.); arXiv:2311.15408 (van den Berg, Wocjan, 'Techniques for learning sparse Pauli-Lindblad noise models'. Title/authors only (arXiv API); candidate entanglement-free estimator source. NOT READ.); arXiv:2206.06362 (Chen, Liu, Otten, Seif, Fefferman, Jiang, 'The learnability of Pauli noise'. Title/authors only: SPAM makes some Pauli-noise parameters unidentifiable for entanglement-free learners; needed to define what the noisy-SPAM arm can compare. NOT READ.); arXiv:2505.22629 (E. H. Chen, S. Chen, Fischer, Eddins, Govia, Mitchell et al., 'Disambiguating Pauli noise in quantum computers'. Title/authors only; possibly relevant to SPAM gauge in the noisy arm. NOT READ.)
