## T9. Theory and miscellaneous (trainability, oracle sketching, RL, error correction)

### I-20261012-07: Variational architecture with provable trainability and no classical simulability

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch A |
| Hypothesis | There exists a parameterized circuit family with gradient variance Omega(1/poly(n)) whose output distribution is #P-hard to simulate, contradicting the trend of arXiv:2312.09121. |
| Mechanism | Seek a barren-plateau-free mechanism (dynamically generated symmetry, classically hard parameter distribution) that does not imply simulability. |
| Data | Theory (task-agnostic). |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | Theoretical: prove or refute for a specific architecture; numerical check at n=6 that gradient variance does not decay exponentially, and compare to a simulable model. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Evidence suggests such architectures are simulable; may be impossible. |
| References cited | 2312.09121, 1803.11173 |
| Reference checks | 2312.09121: **OK** (Exists (Cerezo et al.). It does document the trend, but it is explicitly scoped to simulability of LOSS functions Tr[U rho U^dag O] (CSIM/QESIM), is 'not a theorem', and itself gives an explicit (contrived, cryptographic) non-concentrated loss outside QESIM (Supp. Info. B, p.32). So 'contradicting the trend' by a mere existence proof is already done there.); 1803.11173: **OK** (McClean et al., barren plateaus for random PQCs (abstract read); used generically, correctly.) |
| Technical problems | Conflates two notions: the BP-vs-simulability conjecture concerns classical estimation of the loss (expectation values), not sampling the output distribution. With 'output distribution #P-hard to simulate' the hypothesis is trivially satisfied by known BP-free models: O(log n)-depth local-cost circuits have Omega(1/poly) gradient variance (2001.00550) while constant-depth circuits are already hard to simulate/sample classically (quant-ph/0205133, unless BQP is in AM), and their local loss is light-cone simulable (2312.09121 Table 2).; '#P-hard to simulate' (exact strong simulation) is the wrong hardness notion for a learning model; approximate sampling or loss-estimation hardness would be needed.; No specific architecture or parameter distribution is given, so there is nothing to prove or refute; the 'dynamically generated symmetry / classically hard parameter distribution' mechanism is a wish, not a construction.; A numerical gradient-variance check at n = 6 cannot distinguish polynomial from exponential decay and says nothing about classical simulability, which is asymptotic; at n <= 10 every model is exactly simulable. |
| Kill-criteria check | K6, K7 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2312.09121 (Perspective + case-by-case evidence; Supp. Info. B already gives a BP-free, non-QESIM (cryptographic) loss.); arXiv:2509.09033 (Generative models that are trainable, BP-free and hard to sample classically (68-qubit run) - the generative version already exists.); arXiv:2607.24686 (Stacked free-fermion LCUs: variance Omega(1/(n k^{3l})) vs best classical simulation O(k^{2l} n^3), a tunable trade-off.); arXiv:2507.06344 (Taylor surrogate; super-polynomial complexity outside near-Clifford regions with preliminary evidence of non-exponential gradient decay.); arXiv:2001.00550 (Local-cost shallow circuits: at worst polynomially vanishing gradients.); quant-ph/0205133 (Constant-depth circuits not efficiently classically simulable unless BQP is in AM.) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | As stated the hypothesis is either trivially true (output-distribution hardness is not what Cerezo et al. conjecture about) or already in the cited paper (its Supp. Info. B builds a BP-free, non-QESIM loss); no architecture is named and an n=6 numerical check cannot decide an asymptotic simulability question. |

### I-20261012-08: Exponential size advantage on massive classical data via quantum oracle sketching

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch A |
| Hypothesis | A quantum computer with O(log N) qubits performs classification/dimension reduction on N classical samples with exponentially fewer parameters (smaller size) than any classical machine. |
| Mechanism | Zhao et al. claim quantum oracle sketching (superposition access without QRAM) plus classical shadows builds a succinct model; the advantage is said to hold even if BPP=BQP. |
| Data | Classical, massive (N up to 1e6, d~10). |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | N=1e4 samples in R^4, 4-6 qubits, oracle sketching + shadow readout, linear classifier or PCA; baselines: linear SVM on full data, PCA, random projections; metrics: accuracy/reconstruction and parameter count; success: same accuracy with <=100 parameters vs >=1e3, or N=1e3 vs 1e5. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Advantage is for large N; at 1e4 classical is cheap. |
| References cited | 2604.07639, 2305.10310, 1807.04271, 1910.06151 |
| Reference checks | 2604.07639: **OK** (Exists (Zhao, Zlokapa, Neven, Babbush, Preskill, McClean, Huang). Partial full text read. The attributed mechanism (QOS gives superposition access without QRAM; interferometric classical shadows; advantage independent of BPP = BQP and of classical time) is in the paper. But the card misreads the parameters: the advantage is poly(log D) logical qubits vs Omega(D^0.99) classical registers, where D is the FEATURE dimension, at the same number of samples. It is machine size (memory), not parameter count. The authors' numerics never simulate QOS: its accuracy is taken as the exact LS-SVM solution.); 2305.10310: **OK** (Jaques & Rattew QRAM survey/critique; cited as context for why QRAM-free loading matters - correct.); 1807.04271: **OK** (Tang dequantization; context, correct.); 1910.06151: **OK** (Chia et al. QSVT dequantization framework; context, correct (QOS's claim is about space, not time, so dequantization of time does not directly apply).) |
| Technical problems | Misreads N: in Zhao et al. N is the number of items and D the number of features. The proposed test (N = 1e4 samples in R^4) has D = 4, where any classical learner stores the model in about 5 floats. No space separation can exist.; The success criterion '<= 100 parameters vs >= 1e3' is meaningless: a linear SVM on R^4 has 5 parameters and PCA on R^4 has 16 numbers. The paper's measure is peak memory (logical qubits vs stored registers), not parameter count.; 'O(log N) qubits' should be poly(log D). Their numerics use about 45-60 logical qubits (their Eq. A9), far beyond the 4-6 qubits proposed and beyond the 10-qubit limit, so the quantum side cannot be simulated at a scale where the claim means anything.; A classical simulation cannot witness a space separation anyway: the simulator itself uses 2^n memory. Only the classical side can be tested meaningfully, as the authors themselves did.; The theorem's classical lower bound holds at the SAME sample count as QOS and on worst-case Forrelation-embedded instances. It says nothing about natural datasets, so natural-data evidence depends entirely on the strength of the classical baselines. |
| Kill-criteria check | K4 (of the original paper's numerics, which is what the sharpened test probes), K6 (original minimal test only) |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2604.07639 (The source: unconditional space separation; real-data numerics vs model-building streaming baselines (feature hashing, sparse JL, AWM-Sketch, MISSION) report >10^4 registers needed vs <60 qubits.); arXiv:2609.36479 (QOS size estimate on network-security data: vs a streaming proxy the advantage is 'more nuanced' because feature filtering shrinks the streaming dimension; no per-test-point estimator.); arXiv:2609.13549 (Offline QOS realization; circuits too deep for current hardware.); arXiv:2610.03645 (Independent unconditional quantum space advantages in random-order streams, engineered problems.) |
| Status | **SHORTLISTED (weakened in adversarial review: apply the required fixes before building)** |
| Decision note | The card's minimal test is unworkable (d = 4 features has no space separation; parameter counts are meaningless), but the mechanism is real and recent (Zhao et al. 2026) and its natural-data evidence has an untested gap: all classical baselines build a D'-dimensional model while the task, as the authors define it, is a per-test-point prediction. Shortlisted as a killing experiment with a new, stronger per-test-point classical baseline (adds a testable element beyond Zhao et al. and Li et al., so K7 does not apply). |
| Decisive test | Zhao et al.'s own protocol (20 Newsgroups, TF-IDF, lambda = 1, 100 random category pairs, 5-fold CV; Dorothea, lambda = 200). The quantum side is credited, as in their paper, with the exact LS-SVM accuracy at S_Q = 2*ceil(log2(N+2D)) + ceil(log2(s+1)) + 4 logical qubits. The classical side is a per-test-point chain estimator of the degree-d Chebyshev LS-SVM score, with a capped register budget B and at most N*kappa^2/epsilon rows per test point. Kill the natural-data size claim on a dataset if, under DESIGN.md section 6, the exact LS-SVM does not beat the estimator for some B <= 10*S_Q. A scratch pilot made it plausible: degree 2 agreed with exact LS-SVM on 99.6% of test points, needing about 7e5 rows per test point against a QOS lower bound of about 1.5e7. |

### I-20261012-16: Variational quantum policy gradient for controlling small quantum systems

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch B |
| Hypothesis | A variational quantum agent using parameter-shift gradients beats classical NN policies (DQN) at controlling quantum state transitions under decoherence, with 10x fewer interactions. |
| Mechanism | Claims parameter-shift exact gradients avoid approximation errors of model-free classical RL. |
| Data | Hybrid: rewards from a simulated 4-qubit open system. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 4-qubit gate-calibration task; variational policy vs tuned DQN; metric: target fidelity vs environment interactions; success: 10x fewer interactions. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Barren plateaus in policy parameters. |
| References cited | reference unknown |
| Reference checks | reference unknown: **NOT VERIFIED** (No reference supplied. Closest verified prior work listed below.) |
| Technical problems | Wrong mechanism: parameter-shift gives exact derivatives of the policy's expectation values, but a classical NN policy has exact gradients by backpropagation too. The sample cost of model-free RL comes from the variance of returns (REINFORCE/advantage estimates), not from approximating d(policy)/d(theta).; With finite shots the PQC policy's action probabilities, and hence its log-likelihood gradients, are themselves estimated. At equal environment interactions the quantum agent's gradients are noisier, not cleaner.; Fair access: both agents receive identical classical observations and rewards; the PQC agent gets no coherent access to the controlled system. The only known RL quantum speed-ups (quadratic) need superposition access to the environment (1610.08251; experiment 2103.06294). Without it there is no information-theoretic route to '10x fewer interactions'.; Baseline choice: DQN is value-based and ill-suited to continuous control. Fair baselines are tuned PPO/SAC, CMA-ES and, since the 4-qubit open-system model is known in simulation, model-based optimal control (GRAPE/Krotov), which needs zero environment interactions.; A 4-qubit policy circuit is trivially classically simulable, so any 'win' would only be an inductive-bias statement about a specific function class (K1/K3). |
| Kill-criteria check | K1, K3, K4, K7 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2203.10591 (HEA PQC policies match or beat typical classical NNs in standard environments and quantum control 'with a fraction of the parameters' (parameter count is not a fair metric).); arXiv:2103.05577 (PQC policies solve RL benchmarks; separation only on a discrete-log engineered task.); arXiv:2103.15084 (PQC deep Q-learning vs DQN with extensive hyperparameter search.); arXiv:2502.04909 (Benchmark of QRL classes; advantage beyond artificial problems uncertain.); arXiv:2511.17112 (Controlled PPO comparison; entanglement can hurt.); arXiv:1610.08251 (Quadratic RL improvement requires quantum (coherent) agent-environment interaction.); arXiv:2103.06294 (Experimental learning-time speed-up via quantum channel to environment.); arXiv:2601.18953 (Review: classical RL agents already control quantum devices.) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | No mechanism for fewer environment interactions (both agents see the same classical observations/rewards; parameter-shift exactness is irrelevant since classical backprop is exact too), single untuned-class baseline (DQN), and PQC policies incl. for quantum control are already benchmarked (2203.10591, 2103.05577, 2103.15084, 2502.04909). |

### I-20261012-32: Syndrome-conditioned classification of logical states in a small code

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Proposed by | external batch D |
| Hypothesis | A syndrome-conditioned logical classifier beats an unencoded quantum classifier and tuned classical decoders at noisy logical-state classification below threshold. |
| Mechanism | Error correction preserves quantum information; question is whether it improves a learning task beyond overhead. |
| Data | Quantum: noisy logical states of the 5-qubit code. |
| Minimal test (as proposed) | 5-qubit code with syndrome ancillas (<=10 qubits); learned recovery vs unencoded classifier and MLP/GBT/optimal decoders; logical classification error vs depolarizing rate. |
| Main risk (as proposed) | Classical decoders may match; overhead erases gain. |
| References cited | 2408.13687 |
| Reference checks | 2408.13687: **OK** (Exists (Google Quantum AI / Acharya et al.). Surface-code memories below threshold: d=7, 0.143%/cycle, Lambda = 2.14 (abstract read). It supports only the generic premise that QEC can work below threshold. It concerns neither the 5-qubit code, nor logical-state classification, nor learned decoders.) |
| Technical problems | Under i.i.d. Pauli noise, the noisy logical states are block-diagonal in syndrome sectors, and within each sector they are diagonal in the basis {E\|0_L>, E\|1_L>}. The Helstrom-optimal measurement is therefore syndrome measurement plus maximum-likelihood coset decision plus logical readout, i.e. a 16-entry lookup table that a classical computer evaluates exactly. 'Beats tuned classical decoders' is impossible by construction.; For non-Pauli noise, coherent (channel-adapted) recovery can beat syndrome-plus-Pauli correction. But the optimum is a 32-dimensional SDP solved classically (quant-ph/0703274 does this for [5,1,3]), so 'learning' it adds nothing testable.; 'Below threshold' is undefined for a single fixed distance-3 code: thresholds are properties of code families. For one code only a pseudo-threshold exists.; Encoded vs unencoded classifier measures QEC break-even (different physical resources: 5 data qubits + 4 ancillas vs 1 qubit), not a learning advantage.; Noiseless syndrome ancillas would ignore fault-tolerant syndrome extraction, i.e. noise at the scale where the idea claims to win (K5). |
| Kill-criteria check | K7, K5 |
| Closest prior work (verified) | arXiv:2607.10707 (VQC vs Meta-MLP decoders across FiveQubit/Steane/planar codes: VQC worse in all regimes (0.940 vs 0.9993 teacher-label accuracy in interpolation).); quant-ph/0703274 (SDP-optimal encoding/recovery for 5-qubit codes vs standard [5,1,3] recovery - optimum is classically computable.); arXiv:2202.00555 (Quantum autoencoders learn active QEC strategies.); arXiv:1810.03787 (QCNN-optimised QEC for a given error model.); arXiv:1711.02249 (QVECTOR variational device-tailored QEC (3 and 5 qubits).); arXiv:2506.11552 (Variational QEC via distinguishability loss.); arXiv:1610.04238 (Classical neural decoders for stabilizer codes.) |
| Status | **REJECTED** |
| Decision note | The ceiling is known and classical: for Pauli noise the 16-entry maximum-likelihood syndrome lookup table equals the Helstrom optimum, so no learned model can beat it. For non-Pauli noise the optimal recovery is a classical SDP at 5 qubits (quant-ph/0703274). VQC decoders on the five-qubit code have already been benchmarked and lose to an MLP (2607.10707). |

### T9 best version: Per-test-point low-memory classical baseline vs the quantum oracle sketching size claim

*Kind:* killing experiment. *Built from:* I-20261012-08.

- **Hypothesis:** This hypothesis is the one the experiment tries to establish, which would kill the claim: on Zhao et al.'s own natural-data protocol (20 Newsgroups, Dorothea), a classical per-test-point streaming estimator matches the exact LS-SVM accuracy that QOS is credited with. The estimator is a degree-d Chebyshev polynomial in the Gram matrix, estimated by chains of random rows. It would use at most 10x as many registers as QOS has logical qubits and fewer rows than one QOS circuit run. If so, the reported 4-6 orders of magnitude memory advantage comes from model-building classical baselines, not from the data.
- **Mechanism:** Zhao et al. (2604.07639) prove that classical machines need Omega(D^0.99) memory at the SAME sample count as QOS, on worst-case Forrelation-embedded instances. On natural data, all their classical baselines (feature hashing, sparse JL, AWM-Sketch, MISSION) store a D'-dimensional weight vector, so their memory is at least D'. Yet the task as they define it is to predict the label of ONE given test point, and the quantum procedure is rerun per test point. They excluded per-test-point low-space classical algorithms (their App. G) because for kappa = polylog(N) those need superpolynomially many samples. For well-conditioned natural data, a low-degree polynomial suffices. Write score = k'^T p_d(K + lambda I) y, with K = X X^T and k' = X x'. Each monomial k'^T K^r y is the mean of N^{r+1} <x',x_i0><x_i0,x_i1>...<x_i(r-1),x_ir> y_ir over r+1 i.i.d. rows. A streaming machine computes it holding only x', the previous row and d+1 accumulators (the Ulam-von Neumann random-walk idea; no arXiv reference verified). The estimator's variance grows roughly geometrically with r. It is expected to stay moderate on natural data and to explode on the hard instances, consistent with the theorem. The quantum claim survives this baseline only if natural data need a high degree, or a variance larger than the QOS sample budget.
- **Data:** 20 Newsgroups (scikit-learn, subset 'all', TF-IDF with English stop words, lambda = 1, 100 random category pairs, 5-fold stratified CV), exactly as in Zhao et al. App. A / Fig. 4a. Dorothea (UCI, 10^5 binary features, lambda = 200). IMDb (lambda = 10) is optional if the download is allowed. Primary scaling is raw TF-IDF; the ||X|| = 1 scaling is secondary, because the paper's text does not state its normalisation (the pilot found kappa about 34 raw vs 2 normalised). Classical data only.
- **Quantum model:** QOS is not simulated: it needs about 45-60 logical qubits. As in Zhao et al., it is credited with the exact full-feature LS-SVM decision sign(x'^T (X^T X + lambda I)^{-1} X^T y): noiseless, fault-tolerant and converged, its most favourable case. Size S_Q = 2*ceil(log2(N+2D)) + ceil(log2(s+1)) + 4 logical qubits (their Eq. A9; about 47 for a 20NG pair). Per-test-point sample need is lower-bounded by M_Q = N*kappa^2/epsilon rows, with epsilon = 0.1 and QSVT degree >= kappa. That is about 1.5e7 rows for a raw 20NG pair, before Hadamard-test repetitions or amplitude amplification, which only increase it.
- **Classical baselines:** (1) Chain estimator C(d, B). Degree d in {0,1,2,3,4}, tuned by 3-fold inner CV on each training fold (same folds as all models). Register cap B in {64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, uncapped}. B counts every stored index, value and accumulator, including the test vector (conservative against the classical side); rows longer than the cap are stored by unbiased importance sparsification. The Chebyshev interval is [lambda, lambda + ||K||_F], with ||K||_F estimated from the stream by 2-row chains; the exact lambda_max is a sensitivity check. Rows are split across terms by Neyman allocation. (2) Zhao et al.'s model-building baselines, reproduced: feature hashing + exact ridge and sparse JL at D' = 2^4 to 2^17 (memory D'), to check protocol agreement. (3) The degree-0 centroid score sign(sum_i y_i <x', x_i>), with memory equal to the test vector plus O(1), as a floor.
- **Access model:** Both learners see the same i.i.d. stream of uniformly random training rows z = (i, x_i, y_i) and the same single test vector x'. Both may run per test point, and neither has to output a reusable model, which is Zhao et al.'s own task definition. Classical time is unlimited. Classical rows per test point are capped at M_Q, a LOWER bound on QOS's need, so the classical side never gets more samples. Memory uses Zhao et al.'s units: classical registers (word-RAM) vs logical qubits. The current input row is excluded for both sides; the classical test vector is counted. Both sides get classical data only: no quantum data and no coherent access to the world. So this is the measure-free classical-data setting (M1/M7), not an M8 'learning from experiments' separation. Nothing is handicapped on the quantum side: it gets its idealised terminal accuracy.
- **Metric:** Test accuracy (5-fold CV, averaged over category pairs). Sign agreement with the exact LS-SVM. Peak registers. Rows consumed per test point (median and 90th percentile). The ratio B*/S_Q, where B* is the smallest register cap meeting the rule. Expected accuracy is computed from the analytic first and second moments of the chain estimator for every test point, and checked by direct Monte Carlo on 5 pairs.
- **Success rule:** Pre-registered. For each dataset and cap B, compare C(B) with the exact LS-SVM over the 5 folds using DESIGN.md section 6: the exact LS-SVM 'beats' C(B) iff it is higher on >= 4/5 folds and the mean paired difference exceeds its standard deviation. B* is the smallest B at which the exact LS-SVM does not beat C(B) with rows <= M_Q. Outcomes per dataset: KILLED if B* <= 10*S_Q (about 470 registers for 20NG). PARTIAL if 10*S_Q < B* (only some larger cap, possibly uncapped, works); report the shrunken ratio B*/S_Q. SURVIVES if even the uncapped estimator is beaten within M_Q rows. The study-level verdict 'Zhao et al.'s natural-data size evidence is a baseline artefact' requires KILLED on >= 2 of the datasets run; 'survives a per-test-point baseline' requires SURVIVES on >= 2. Raw scaling decides; normalised scaling is reported beside it.
- **Milestone:** M1 (natural-data evidence for an unconditional classical-data separation), secondarily M7 (QOS as the QRAM-free route to data access)
- **Kill check:** A scratch pilot was run (not a result). Setup: 3-8 random 20NG pairs, lambda = 1, raw TF-IDF. Findings: exact LS-SVM accuracy 0.9905; the degree-2 Chebyshev score agreed with the exact sign on 99.6% of test points (degree 8: 99.9%; degree-0 centroid: 98.0%, accuracy 0.978). The chain estimator needed a median of 7.4e5 and a 90th percentile of 3.4e6 rows per test point at degree 2, vs about 1.5e7 for one QOS shot. Direct Monte Carlo confirmed the analytic variance: P(sign correct) 0.970 simulated vs 0.966 predicted at 1e6 rows. Mean row nnz was 103, max 4272, so the cap and sparsification rule matters. Abort or reframe if any of the following holds on the full protocol: (a) no d <= 4 reaches >= 99% sign agreement; (b) median rows exceed M_Q, in which case the baseline cannot match and the run would only confirm the claim; (c) our exact LS-SVM accuracies differ by > 2 pp from Zhao et al.'s reported terminal accuracies (protocol mismatch).
- **Cost estimate:** New module src/qmlrc/streaming.py, about 300 lines: 20NG/Dorothea loaders, exact LS-SVM, Chebyshev-to-monomial expansion, analytic chain moments, vectorised Monte Carlo chain simulator, register accounting, feature-hashing reproduction. About 120 lines of tests: unbiasedness on a toy matrix, analytic vs simulated variance, register counter, S_Q formula. About 60 lines to register three experiments (20NG raw, 20NG normalised, Dorothea). CPU: the scratch pilot took 75 s for 8 pairs x 5 folds x 2 scalings x 6 degrees, so about 8 min per dataset-scaling at 100 pairs, plus about 3 min of Monte Carlo verification. Total about 25-30 CPU-min, each experiment <= 10 min. No qubits simulated; numpy/scipy/scikit-learn only.
- **Win teaches:** Here a WIN means the quantum claim SURVIVES. If even the uncapped per-test-point estimator cannot match the exact LS-SVM within the QOS sample budget, Zhao et al.'s natural-data size advantage survives a stronger classical baseline that their paper did not test. That would be the first case in this repository of a QML resource advantage on natural classical data surviving a tuned, access-matched classical baseline. It would raise M1's natural-data evidence. The advantage would still be idealised: fault-tolerant, noiseless, runtime ignored.
- **Loss teaches:** Here a LOSS means the claim is KILLED. If a chain estimator with <= 10x S_Q registers matches, the reported 4-6 orders of magnitude on natural data come from comparing a per-test-point quantum procedure with model-building classical algorithms. QOS's unconditional advantage then lives only on worst-case Forrelation-embedded instances, like every other M1 separation, and the classical-data estimate (about 5%) stands. A PARTIAL outcome quantifies how far the memory ratio shrinks, a number worth reporting back to the authors.
- **Closest prior work:** arXiv:2604.07639 (Source claim; classical baselines all store a D'-dimensional model; low-space per-test-point classical algorithm excluded (App. G).); arXiv:2609.36479 (QOS size estimate on network data; feature filtering already erodes the advantage vs a streaming proxy.); arXiv:2609.13549 (QOS practical requirements; circuits too deep for current hardware.); arXiv:2610.03645 (Unconditional quantum space advantages in random-order streams, engineered problems.)

**Adversarial review: WEAKENED.** Verdict: WEAKENED. The experiment has a real target. Zhao et al.'s natural-data numerics compare a per-test-point QOS, credited with the exact LS-SVM, against classical baselines that each store a D'-dimensional model. A randomised version of the low-space polynomial solver they set aside in App. G is a legitimate, stronger baseline, and nobody has run it. Simple per-test-point baselines do not close the gap: on 12 pairs x 5 folds, centroid, Rocchio and kNN lose 0.9 to 1.7 pp to the exact LS-SVM on every fold. As pre-registered, though, the outcome is decided by conventions, not by the data: - **Sample budget.** The classical sample cap M_Q is a constant-free heuristic, not a lower bound. Equally defensible readings (kappa vs kappa_reg; raw vs the paper's own ||X|| = 1) move it by 30x to 290x, and the verdict flips with them. - **Asymmetric treatment.** QOS gets infinite-sample accuracy while the classical side is evaluated at finite rows. - **Memory.** The pilot's "about 400 registers" is a mean. Peak memory is 3.5e3 to 8.8e3, and the test vector is charged only to the classical side, so the KILLED vs PARTIAL line at 10 x S_Q is set by bookkeeping. - **Chebyshev interval.** The pre-registered Frobenius interval breaks the claimed 99% degree-2 agreement. - **Decision rule.** Averaged over 100 pairs, the §6 rule flags 0.2 pp deficits. - **Datasets.** The headline datasets (IMDb, PBMC68k) are optional or absent. The fixes would turn it into a decisive experiment: a memory-sample frontier, QOS simulated at matched sample budgets, symmetric peak-memory accounting, a non-inferiority margin, and IMDb included. It would then also be worth reporting to the authors. Scratch checks (not results): /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-quantum-ml-reality-check/3242e8f8-b193-546c-b381-3fe888e7979d/scratchpad/round01/t9_review/check.py (S_Q, kappa, M_Q, peak registers, interval and degree effects, expected accuracy at a given budget) and check2.py (centroid, Rocchio and kNN vs exact LS-SVM). No file in the repository was modified.

- *Already done:* Not done as an experiment, so K7 does not kill it. Searches: 4 alphaXiv discover queries; 1 successful arXiv API query, abs:"oracle sketching", which returned only 2604.07639, 2609.13549, 2609.36479 and one unrelated hit (a second arXiv call and the id_list resolutions hit HTTP 429 after the one permitted retry). None of these runs a per-test-point low-memory classical estimator against QOS's natural-data numerics. The idea itself is close to things already in print. (1) 2604.07639 App. G (full text read) gives the deterministic version: evaluate p_d(A)b in polylog space by recursive recomputation. They exclude it from all numerics only because its runtime is N^{O(log d)} when d = polylog(N). App. G also says the paper's own Forrelation-NOPE hard instances 'can indeed be solved with poly(log N) space and O~(N^2) samples'. So the static-task separation is a separation at equal sample count (O~(N) vs O~(N^2)), not an absolute one. (2) The randomised chain estimator is the classical Ulam-von Neumann method; 2407.16661 is now a verified reference, read in full, including its convergence condition rho(A^2/P) < 1. What the proposal adds: an empirical test of whether fixed low degree plus Monte Carlo makes App. G's excluded route practical on natural data. That is testable and new, so it is not a duplicate. It must cite App. G as the closest prior and present the estimator as a randomised App. G, not as an unknown baseline.
- *Classical can match:* Simple per-test-point baselines do NOT close the gap, so the chain estimator really is the right attack. My scratch check (12 random 20NG pairs x 5 folds, raw TF-IDF, lambda = 1) gave exact LS-SVM 0.9925. The baselines were: degree-0 centroid 0.9833, class-balanced Rocchio 0.9816, streaming kNN 0.9752 to 0.9839 (k = 5, 15, 45). Exact beats each on 5/5 folds by 0.9 to 1.7 pp, with fold-difference SD of only 0.07 to 0.23 pp. Within the chain family, results depend on the polynomial (4 pairs, one fold): - Exact-lambda_max interval, d = 2: sign agreement 0.985 to 1.000. - The proposal's PRIMARY Frobenius interval: ||K||_F/lambda_max = 1.8 to 2.2. At d = 2, agreement falls to 0.979 to 0.997 (mean about 0.985), below the proposal's own abort line (a) of >= 99%. Reaching about 0.993 needs d = 3, with median rows 1.2e6 to 1.9e6 and 99th percentile 2e7 to 7e8, above M_Q. - Low-degree Chebyshev is non-monotone: d = 1 can be 4.5 pp worse than d = 0. The proposal also leaves cheap gains to the classical side unused: control variates (the degree-0 term costs ~1e2 to 3e4 rows), reusing each row across terms, and regenerative or importance variants (2407.16661). Without them the classical side is under-powered, which biases toward a spurious SURVIVES/PARTIAL.
- *Access model issue:* The setting is the right one: classical data only, the same i.i.d. stream of whole rows (Task F.9 delivers z = (i, x_i, y_i) as full s-slot rows), per-test-point prediction, word-RAM registers vs logical qubits. It is M1/M7, not M8. But three asymmetries remain, and they set the verdict. (1) Samples. The classical side is evaluated at finite rows (capped at M_Q, about one QOS circuit run, no Hadamard-test shots). QOS is credited with exact, infinite-sample LS-SVM accuracy. Small-margin test points are expensive for both sides: QOS needs ~1/overlap^2 shots, each costing a full run. So a SURVIVES outcome would be produced by the asymmetry itself, and 'win_teaches' (first resource advantage surviving an access-matched baseline) does not follow. (2) Test vector. Eq. A9 counts no storage for x'; the classical side is charged 2*nnz(x'), median about 180 to 260 registers, i.e. 4 to 5 x S_Q. That is half the 10 x S_Q kill line, so KILLED vs PARTIAL is decided by bookkeeping. (3) Normalisation. Task F.9 and Lemma F.14 fix ||X|| = 1, which the proposal says the paper never states. Raw-scaling-primary makes M_Q about 290x larger (kappa about 34 vs 2), which is the convention most favourable to the classical sample cap. Minor points. The degree d is tuned offline with full memory; declare it as an offline hyperparameter like lambda. And 2610.03645 notes that Zhao et al.'s classical lower bound is proven for a correlated, block-structured stream, not i.i.d. uniform order. The mechanism's 'at the SAME sample count' claim should say so.
- *Feasibility:* It runs: no qubits are simulated; numpy/scikit-learn only; my 4-pair x 2-scaling x 2-interval x 5-degree check ran in minutes. But it is not decisive as pre-registered, and the budget estimate is optimistic. Decisiveness: (a) The outcome flips with the M_Q convention. At the proposal's N kappa^2/epsilon (0.95e7 to 3.0e7), expected d = 2 accuracy is within 0.1 pp of exact on 3 of 4 pairs but 1.5 pp below on pair (5,19). At N kappa_reg^2/epsilon (3.5e5 to 6.8e5, an equally defensible reading) it is 0.83 to 0.97 vs exact 0.99+. Under the paper's own ||X|| = 1 scaling, M_Q is only 5e4 to 6e4 and d = 1 loses up to 0.6 pp. (b) Averaged over 100 pairs, the §6 rule flags deficits of ~0.2 to 0.3 pp as consistent, so Monte Carlo sign noise in the tail alone yields 'exact beats C(B)'. (c) The pilot reports MEAN memory (about 400). The paper's metric, and the proposal's own, is PEAK: 2*nnz(x') + 2*max row nnz = 3.5e3 to 8.8e3 uncapped (70 to 180 x S_Q). Even mean memory (408 to 571) exceeds 10 x S_Q on 3 of 4 pairs, so KILLED needs sparsification in most chains, and that variance is absent from the pilot's row counts. (d) Dorothea: from memory, NOT VERIFIED here, about 900 binary nonzeros per row. If so, x' alone exceeds 10 x S_Q and KILLED is unreachable, which makes the study-level 'KILLED on >= 2 datasets' nearly unreachable by design. (e) The 4-to-6-orders headline is on IMDb and PBMC68k (Fig. 2), not on 20NG or Dorothea (Fig. 4). IMDb is optional here and at N_train = 40k is heavy for the 10-minute budget. Budget: the 8-min-per-dataset figure omits inner 3-fold CV, the 8-cap sweep with sparsification Monte Carlo, and 14 D' values of hashing reproduction. Expect several times 30 CPU-min unless the number of pairs is cut.
- *Technical errors:* M_Q = N kappa^2/epsilon is called a LOWER bound on QOS's sample need. It is not proven. With kappa = cond(K + lambda I) = kappa_reg^2, it equals N Q0^2/epsilon for Lemma F.14's Q0 = O(kappa_reg^2 log^2(kappa_reg/epsilon)) with the logs dropped. It also drops Theorem D.23's per-query cost O(R N s^5 log^2 N log^4(...)/epsilon), and in 20NG with headers s_col = N (some token appears in every document). It is a constant-free heuristic, and the verdict depends on it.; 'QSVT degree >= kappa' is wrong for the paper's construction. Lemma F.14 block-encodes the augmented matrix A_lambda, so the inversion degree is ~kappa_reg log(kappa_reg/epsilon) with kappa_reg = sqrt(kappa) about 5.8 raw. The second factor kappa_reg comes from fixed-point amplitude amplification. So saying amplitude amplification 'only increases' M_Q double counts.; 'The paper's text does not state its normalisation' is only half true. Task F.9, Theorem F.13 and Lemma F.14 all assume ||X|| = 1 with a normalised lambda. Only the Appendix A numerics leave it unstated.; 'Typical memory is about 400 registers' is a mean. The metric (theirs and the proposal's 'peak registers') is the maximum over the stream: 2*nnz(x') + 2*max_i nnz(x_i) = 3.5e3 to 8.8e3 on the pairs I checked (max row nnz 1651 to 4310).; The pilot's 99.6% degree-2 sign agreement used the exact lambda_max interval. With the pre-registered Frobenius interval, agreement at d = 2 is about 98.5% (0.979 to 0.997), which trips abort criterion (a).; The pilot's row counts are for the uncapped estimator. The pre-registered cap with importance sparsification adds variance that is not in the analytic moments.; Kill-check (c) (a 2 pp tolerance against Zhao et al.'s terminal accuracy) cannot tell the scalings apart: exact accuracy differs by only about 0.5 pp between raw and ||X|| = 1. The paper's accuracies are only in figures (Fig. 4a), not in the text.
- *Required fixes:* Replace the single M_Q cap with a frontier. Sweep the per-test-point budget (1e3 to 1e10 rows) and the register cap, and report accuracy as a function of both. Pre-register the QOS reference budget derived from Lemma F.14 (Q0 = kappa_reg^2 log^2(kappa_reg/epsilon), kappa_reg as in Eq. F50 under the SAME normalisation) and Theorem D.23, as an explicit band (with and without logs; the s^5 factor reported separately), not as a 'lower bound'.; Simulate QOS at finite samples too: ideal oracles, Hadamard-test shot noise on the normalised overlap <x'|w>, each shot costing one run's samples. Compare both sides at the SAME per-test-point sample budget. Drop the 'first resource advantage' reading of SURVIVES unless it holds at matched budgets.; Memory accounting: report PEAK registers. Treat x' symmetrically: exclude it on both sides as Eq. A9 does (primary), and report it included as a sensitivity. Put the sparsification variance into the analytic moments and check it by Monte Carlo.; Make B*/S_Q the primary outcome. Compare it with the paper's own ratio (the D' feature hashing needs for terminal accuracy, divided by S_Q). Drop the arbitrary 10 x S_Q KILLED line, or justify it in bits (registers x 64 vs qubits) and in registers.; Normalisation: make ||X|| = 1 (as in Task F.9 and Lemma F.14) co-primary with raw. Decide which one reproduces the paper by matching the Fig. 4a accuracy to within 0.3 pp, not 2 pp. Compute the quantum reference budget under the same scaling.; Decision rule: with 100 pairs averaged per fold, §6 detects about 0.2 pp. Pre-register a non-inferiority margin (e.g. C(B) 'matches' if the mean paired deficit is < 0.3 to 0.5 pp), or use per-pair paired tests, and report sign agreement.; Spectral interval and degree: either estimate lambda_max from the stream (power-iteration chains, with their row cost charged) or accept the Frobenius interval with d = 3 to 4. Pre-register degree selection as an offline hyperparameter (like lambda).; Give the classical side its strongest estimator: control variates on the degree-0 term, row reuse across terms, and regenerative or importance variants (2407.16661). Otherwise a PARTIAL or SURVIVES result is a baseline artefact in the other direction.; Datasets: include IMDb (lambda = 10), the paper's Fig. 2 headline, as required, subsampled if needed and documented. Check Dorothea's nnz per row before pre-registering; drop it or change the study-level rule if KILLED is structurally unreachable there.; Cite App. G of 2604.07639 as the closest prior work: a deterministic polylog-space p_d(A)b, plus their admission that the hard instances fall to polylog space with O~(N^2) samples. Frame the experiment as a measurement of the memory-sample trade-off on natural data, not an absolute memory test.
- *Further prior work:* arXiv:2604.07639 (Zhao, Zlokapa, Neven, Babbush, Preskill, McClean, Huang. Full text read in parts: Sec. II; App. A; App. B dequantisation/streaming; Task F.9, Thm F.11, F.13; Lemma F.14; Thm D.23 statement; App. G. Findings: Task F.9 fixes ||X|| = 1. Eq. A9 counts no storage for the test vector. Lemma F.14 gives Q0 = O(kappa_reg^2 log^2(kappa_reg/eps)) queries; Thm D.23 gives O(R N s^5 ... /eps) samples per block-encoding query. App. G gives a deterministic polylog-space p_d(A)b solver, excluded from the numerics only for its N^{O(log d)} runtime, and states that the hard instances are solvable with polylog space and O~(N^2) samples. The numerics credit QOS with the exact LS-SVM and say comparisons with other methods are left to future work.); arXiv:2407.16661 (Ghosh, Horesh, Kalantzis, Lu, Nowicki, 'Regenerative Ulam-von Neumann Algorithm' (full text read, most pages). Verified reference for the classical Monte Carlo random-walk linear solver behind the chain estimator, including the convergence condition rho(A^2/P) < 1 and a regenerative variance-reduction variant. Fills the proposal's 'no arXiv reference verified' gap.); arXiv:2610.03645 (Bouland, Ding, Jain, 'Exponential quantum space advantage in random data streams' (pp. 1-5 read). Footnote: Zhao et al.'s classical lower bound is proven for a correlated stream sampled in structured blocks, not an i.i.d., uniformly ordered one. So on the i.i.d. stream used in this experiment, the unconditional bound is not known to apply.); arXiv:2609.36479 (Li, Nehru, Zhang et al. (arXiv API abstract only). A QOS memory proxy beats explicit sparse/QRAM storage, but against a simple streaming proxy the result is 'more nuanced' because feature filtering shrinks the streaming dimension. Closest independent re-examination of QOS memory claims; no per-test-point estimator.); arXiv:2609.13549 (Markidis (arXiv API abstract only). Practical requirements of QOS; compiled circuits too deep for current hardware. No classical-baseline test.)
